x\in(\lambda-\mathcal{G})\mathcal{T}_{f}
. Hence,
\Sigma\circ R(\lambda,\overline{\mathcal{G}})
is contractive on the dense set
(\lambda-\mathcal{G})\mathcal{T}_{f}
and positivity preserving, since both
\Sigma
and
R(\lambda,\overline{\mathcal{G}})
are. It can therefore be uniquely extended to a positivity preserving contractive map on all of
W^{k,1}
which we will call
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}
in the following. As a consequence
(\mathcal{L}_{\delta},\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{L}_{\delta}))
is closable and
\lambda>\omega_{k}
in the resolvent set of the closure. Both facts follow from the representation

(\lambda-\mathcal{L}_{\delta})=(\mathbbm{1}-\delta\Sigma\circ R(\lambda,\overline{\mathcal{G}}))\circ(\lambda-\mathcal{G})

which decomposes
\lambda-\mathcal{L}_{\delta}
into a composition of a closable map with a dense range and a map that is bounded on that range. We further get for the resolvent of the closure

R(\lambda,\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\delta})=R(\lambda,\overline{\mathcal{G}})\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}\delta^{n}\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}^{n}\,,

which immediately lets us conclude that the resolvent is positivity preserving as
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}
and
R(\lambda,\overline{\mathcal{G}})
are. Lastly, we will show that for
\lambda>\omega_{k}

(34)

\norm{R(\lambda,\overline{\cL}_\delta)}_{W^{k,1}\to W^{k,1}}\leq\frac{1}{\lambda-\omega_{k}}\,.

To obtain this inequality we again rearrange Assumption
2
, add non-negative terms, use cyclicity of the trace and that
\tr[\cdot]\leq\norm{\cdot}_{1}
with equality if the argument is positive semi-definite, to conclude that for
x\in(\lambda-\mathcal{L}_{r})\mathcal{T}_{f}
,
x
positive semi-definite,

\norm{R(\lambda,\overline{\cL}_\delta) x}_{W^{k,1}}\leq\frac{1}{\lambda-\omega_{k}}\norm{x}_{W^{k,1}}\,.

We again extend the above bound to all
x\in(\lambda-\mathcal{L}_{\delta})\mathcal{T}_{f}
analogously to step 2 in the proof of Lemma
3.4
. Using that
(\lambda-\mathcal{L}_{\delta})\mathcal{T}_{f}
is dense then gives
Equation

34
. Employing Theorem
2.7
, we get that indeed for all
\delta\in(0,1)
the closure of
(\mathcal{L}_{\delta},\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{L}_{\delta}))
generates a strongly continuous semigroup which is positivity preserving since the resolvent is and satisfies the claimed bound. To now fill the gap between
0
and the
\{k_{r}\}_{r\in\mathbbm{N}}
respectively, we interpolate between the semigroups (q.v. Lemma
E.4
), obtaining
e^{t\omega_{k}}
where
\omega_{k}=\frac{k_{r_{1}}-k}{k_{r_{1}}-k_{r_{0}}}\omega_{k_{r_{0}}}+\frac{k-k_{r_{0}}}{k_{r_{1}}-k_{r_{0}}}\omega_{k_{r_{1}}}
for an
r
such that
k_{r_{0}}\leq k<k_{r_{1}}
, as the bound of the interpolated semigroups. Now that we have the result for all
k\geq 0
we can employ Lemma
E.5
and take the limit
\delta\to 1
to obtain the assertion. The contractivity and trace-preserving property of the semigroup in the case
k=0
just follows from the GKSL form of
(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{L}))
, i.e.
\tr[\mathcal{L}(x)]=0
for
x\in\mathcal{T}_{f}
, or put differently Assumption
1
.
∎

3.2
Bosonic evolution systems

Next, we consider time-dependent generators in GKSL form. For this, we modify Assumptions
1
and
2
in the following way:

Assumption 3

The operator
(\mathcal{L}_{s},\mathcal{T}_{f})
has
\operatorname{GKSL}
form, i.e. for
x\in\mathcal{T}_{f}
and
s\in[0,\infty)

(35)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{s}:\mathcal{T}_{f}\to\mathcal{T}_{f}\quad x\mapsto\mathcal{L}_{s}(x)

\displaystyle=-i[H(s),x]+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{K}L_{j}(s)xL_{j}^{\dagger}(s)-\frac{1}{2}\{L_{j}^{\dagger}(s)L_{j}(s),x\}

\displaystyle\coloneqq G(s)x+xG^{\dagger}(s)+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{K}L_{j}(s)xL_{j}^{\dagger}(s)\,,

where
K\in\mathbb{N}
,
G(s)=-iH(s)-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{K}L_{j}^{\dagger}(s)L_{j}(s)
, and
H(s)\coloneqq p_{H(s)}(a,a^{\dagger}),L_{j}(s)\coloneqq p_{j,s}(a,a^{\dagger})
are polynomials of the creation and annihilation operators with time-dependent, continuous coefficients. Again,
d_{H}\coloneqq\sup_{s\geq 0}\,\deg(p_{H(s)})<\infty
,
d_{j}\coloneqq\sup_{s\geq 0}\deg(p_{j,s})<\infty
, and
d\coloneqq\max\{d_{1},...,d_{K},d_{H}\}
.

The next assumption will lead to the evolution system being Sobolev preserving, which allows us not only to prove the existence and uniqueness of the evolution generated by (
27
) but further to conduct a perturbation analysis as well as to extend our results to the case of a time-dependent Master equation:

Assumption 4

There exists a non-negative sequence
\{k_{r}\}_{r\in\mathbbm{N}}\rightarrow\infty
s.t. for all
r\in\mathbbm{N}
there exist
\omega_{k_{r}}\geq 0
such that for all
s\in\mathbbm{R}_{+}
and
x\in\mathcal{T}_{f}
positive semi-definite,

(36)

\tr[\mathcal{L}_{s}(x)(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2}]\leq\omega_{k_{r}}\tr[x(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2}]\,.

Note that the coefficients
\omega_{k_{r}}
are independent of
s
.

Under the above assumptions we can state the generation theorem for evolution systems as follows:

Theorem 3.5

(Generation of bosonic evolution systems)

Let
(\mathcal{L}_{s},\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{L}_{s}))_{s\in[0,\infty)}
be a family of operators that fulfill Assumption
3
and Assumption
4
. Then
(\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{s},\mathcal{D}(\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{s}))_{s\in\mathbbm{R}_{+}}
gives rise to a unique evolution system
(\mathcal{P}_{t,s})_{0\leq s\leq t}
on
W^{k,1}
for all
k\geq 0
with the following properties

1.

\mathcal{P}_{t,s}(W^{k+4d,1})\subseteq W^{k+4d,1}
for all
0\leq s\leq t

2.

For any
x\in W^{k+4d,1}
, the family
(\mathcal{P}_{t,s}(x))_{0\leq s\leq t}
is the unique solution to the initial value problem

(37)

\frac{d}{dt}x(t)=\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{t}(x(t))\qquad t\in[s,\infty),\;x(s)=x\,.

For
k=0
, the evolution system is contractive and trace-preserving.

Proof.

We assume w.l.o.g. that
s\in[0,1]
is fixed since the same argument works for all compact intervals. Theorem
3.1
shows that
(\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{s},\mathcal{D}(\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{s}))
generates an
\omega_{k}
-quasi-contractive semigroup
(\mathcal{P}_{t}^{s})_{t\geq 0}
on
W^{k,1}
. Next, we realize that
W^{k+4d,1}
are
\mathcal{L}_{s}
-admissible subspaces, where we recall that
d
denotes the degree of
\mathcal{L}_{s}
. This already proves assumptions (1) and (2) in Theorem
2.10
. Since the coefficients of the polynomials
p_{H}
and
p_{j}
are continuous and operators of the form

(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/4}a^{j}(a^{\dagger})^{l}(N+\mathbbm{1})^{-(k/4+d)}\,,

for
j+l\leq d
, are bounded (see Lemma
E.2
) w.r.t. the operator norm, we have by Hölder inequality that

s\mapsto(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/4}\mathcal{L}_{s}((N+\mathbbm{1})^{-k/4+d}(\cdot)(N+\mathbbm{1})^{-k/4+d})(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/4}=:\mathcal{A}(s)

is a bounded and uniformly continuous family of operators. Therefore,

s\mapsto\mathcal{L}_{s}\in\mathcal{B}(W^{k,1},W^{k+4d,1})

is uniformly continuous, which proves condition (3) in Theorem
2.10
.
Hence Theorem
2.10
provides the existence and uniqueness of an evolution system on
W^{k,1}
. By repeating the above arguments on
\mathcal{Y}\coloneqq W^{k+4d}
, i.e. by choosing our
\mathcal{L}_{s}
-admissible subspace as
W^{k+8d,1}
, Theorem
2.10
provides existence and uniqueness of a solution on
\mathcal{Y}=W^{k+4d,1}
which agrees with the former one on
W^{k,1}
by the compact embedding of
W^{k+4d,1}
into
W^{k,1}
. Therefore, conditions (4) and (5) are satisfied for the evolution system on
W^{k,1}
and the admissible subspace
\mathcal{Y}=W^{k+4d,1}
, which through Theorem
2.10
proves the claim. Moreover, the evolution system is positivity preserving because it can be constructed by a concatenation of time-independent positivity preserving semigroups (see Theorem
3.1
and
[
54
]
). Contractivity and the property of trace preservation are a consequence of the fact that
\omega_{0}
can be chosen to be
0
.
∎

3.3
Multi-mode extension

This section discusses the extension of Section
3
to the multi-mode setting. Since the details are almost completely analogous to the single-mode situation, we choose to elaborate only at places where some ambiguities might remain. Let us first fix the notations for this setting. We consider the Hilbert space of an
m
-mode system,
m\in\mathbbm{N}
, whose Hilbert space we conveniently denote by
\mathcal{H}_{m}=L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
. We further use
\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H}_{m})
for the bounded,
\mathcal{T}_{1}
for the trace class, and
\mathcal{T}_{1,\operatorname{sa}}
for the self-adjoint trace class operators. Now we define
\mathcal{T}_{f}
to be

\mathcal{T}_{f}\coloneqq\{x=\sum\limits_{\text{finite}}f_{\textbf{n},\textbf{p}}\outerproduct{n_{1}}{p_{1}}\otimes\ldots\otimes\outerproduct{n_m}{p_m}\;:\;f_{\textbf{n},\textbf{p}}\in\mathbbm{C},\;x=x^{\dagger}\}\,,

where
\textbf{n}=(n_{1},\ldots,n_{m})\in\mathbbm{N}^{m}
and
p
analogously function as an index in
f_{\textbf{n},\textbf{p}}
. For
\textbf{k}=(k_{1},\ldots,k_{m})\in\mathbbm{R}_{+}^{m}
, we define
\textbf{k}\prec\textbf{k}^{\prime}
if
k_{j}<k_{j}^{\prime}
for all
j=1,\ldots,m
. Analogously, we define
\preceq
. We set for
\textbf{k}\in\mathbbm{R}_{+}^{m}

(N+\mathbbm{1})^{\textbf{k}}\coloneqq(N_{1}+\mathbbm{1})^{k_{1}}\otimes\ldots\otimes(N_{m}+\mathbbm{1})^{k_{m}},

and with this define
W^{\textbf{k},1}
,
\norm{\,\cdot\,}_{W^{\textbf{k},1}}
. Remark that the latter spaces are Banach spaces and in correspondence to Lemma
2.13
we find:

Lemma 3.6

Let
\textbf{k},\textbf{k}^{\prime}\in\mathbbm{N}^{m}
with
\textbf{k}\prec\textbf{k}^{\prime}
, then

(38)

W^{\textbf{k}^{\prime},1}\Subset W^{\textbf{k},1}\,.

The strategy to prove the above claims is analogous to the single-mode case. Next, we slightly generalize the single-mode results Theorem
2.14
and Definition
2.15
to the multi-mode setting.

Theorem 3.7

(Stein-Weiss theorem for multi-mode Bosonic Sobolev spaces)

Let
\textbf{k}_{0},\textbf{k}_{1}\in\mathbbm{R}_{+}^{m}
,
\textbf{k}_{0}\prec\textbf{k}_{1}
and
T:W^{\textbf{k}_{j},1}\to W^{\textbf{k}_{j},1}
a linear map with
\norm{T}_{W^{\textbf{k}_{j},1}\to W^{\textbf{k}_{j},1}}\leq M_{j}
, bounded by
M_{j}\geq 0
for
j=0,1
respectively. Then for
\theta\in[0,1]
,
T:W^{\textbf{k}_{\theta},1}\to W^{\textbf{k}_{\theta},1}
with
\textbf{k}_{\theta}=(1-\theta)\textbf{k}_{0}+\theta\textbf{k}_{1}
obtained by restriction of the input of
T:W^{\textbf{k}_{0},1}\to W^{\textbf{k}_{0},1}
to
W^{\textbf{k}_{\theta},1}\cap W^{\textbf{k}_{0},1}
, is a well defined bounded linear map with

(39)

\norm{T}_{W^{\textbf{k}_{\theta},1}\to W^{\textbf{k}_{\theta},1}}\leq M_{0}^{(1-\theta)}M_{1}^{\theta}\,.

In the multi-mode setting, we cannot interpolate between the elements of the divergent sequence to obtain
W^{\textbf{k},1}
as admissible subspace for all
0\prec\textbf{k}
but only for elements in the convex hull of the divergent sequence. The property of being Sobolev preserving is again defined for a sequence
\{\textbf{k}_{r}\}_{r\in\mathbbm{N}}
such that
\lim\limits_{r\to\infty}\min\limits_{j=1,\ldots,m}k_{j,r}=\infty
.

Definition 3.8

(Sobolev preserving semigroup/evolution system in multi-mode systems)

Let
(\mathcal{P}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
be a
C_{0}
-semigroup on
\mathcal{T}_{1,\operatorname{sa}}
. We then call
(\mathcal{P}_{t})_{t\geq 0}

Sobolev preserving
if there exists a divergent sequence
\{\textbf{k}_{r}\}_{r\in\mathbbm{N}}\subset\mathbbm{R}_{+}^{m}
, in the sense that
\lim\limits_{r\to\infty}\min\limits_{j=1,\ldots,m}k_{j,r}=\infty
, s.t. for all
r\in\mathbbm{N}
,
W^{\textbf{k}_{r},1}
is an admissible subspace for
(\mathcal{P}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
. Similarly for an evolution system
(\mathcal{P}_{t,s})_{0\leq s\leq t}
on
\mathcal{T}_{1,\operatorname{sa}}
, we call it
(\mathcal{P}_{t,s})_{0\leq s\leq t}

Sobolev preserving
if for all
r\in\mathbbm{N}
,
W^{\textbf{k}_{r},1}
is admissible for
(\mathcal{P}_{t,s})_{0\leq s\leq t}
to
W^{\textbf{k}_{r},1}
.

With these preliminaries in place we can now lift Assumption
1
, Assumption
2
, Assumption
3
, and Assumption
4
.

Assumption 5

The operator
(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{T}_{f})
has
\operatorname{GKSL}
form, i.e. for
x\in\mathcal{T}_{f}
,

(40)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}:\mathcal{T}_{f}\to\mathcal{T}_{f}\quad x\mapsto\mathcal{L}(x)

\displaystyle=-i[H,x]+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{K}L_{j}xL_{j}^{\dagger}-\frac{1}{2}\{L_{j}^{\dagger}L_{j},x\}

\displaystyle\coloneqq Gx+xG^{\dagger}+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{K}L_{j}xL_{j}^{\dagger}\,,

for some
K\in\mathbbm{N}
and with
G=-iH-\frac{1}{2}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{K}L_{j}^{\dagger}L_{j}
, where
\{A,B\}=AB+BA
denotes the anti-commutator of two operators
A,B
on a suitable domain. Further
H
and
L_{j}
are assumed to be polynomials of the creation and annihilation operators, i.e.
H\coloneqq p_{H}(a_{1},a_{1}^{\dagger},\ldots,a_{m},a_{m}^{\dagger})
,
L_{j}\coloneqq p_{j}(a_{1},a_{1}^{\dagger},\ldots,a_{m},a_{m}^{\dagger})
and
H
symmetric. This ensures that
\mathcal{T}_{f}
is invariant under
\mathcal{L}
. We denote the degree of
p_{H}
by
d_{H}\coloneqq\deg p_{H}
, those of
p_{j}
by
d_{j}\coloneqq\deg p_{j}
, and
d\coloneqq\max\{d_{1},\ldots,d_{K},d_{H}\}
.

The second assumption becomes:

Assumption 6

There exists a non-negative sequence
\{\textbf{k}_{r}\}_{r\in\mathbbm{N}}\subset\mathbbm{R}_{+}^{m}
, in the sense that

\lim\limits_{r\to\infty}\min\limits_{j=1,\ldots,m}k_{j,r}=\infty
, s.t. for every
r\in\mathbbm{N}
, there exist
\omega_{\textbf{k}_{r}}\geq 0
such that for all positive semi-definite
x\in\mathcal{T}_{f}

(41)

\tr[\mathcal{L}(x)(N+\mathbbm{1})^{\textbf{k}_{r}/2}]\leq\omega_{\textbf{k}_{r}}\tr[x(N+\mathbbm{1})^{\textbf{k}_{r}/2}]\,.

Then employing the single-mode strategy, we obtain the following theorem.

Theorem 3.9

(Generation of multi-mode bosonic semigroups)

Let
(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{L}))
be an operator defined on
\mathcal{T}_{1,\operatorname{sa}}
. If
(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{L}))
satisfies Assumption
5
and Assumption
6
, then the closure
\overline{\mathcal{L}}
generates a strongly continuous, positivity preserving semigroup
(\mathcal{P}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
on
W^{\textbf{k}_{r},1}
for all
\{\textbf{k}_{r}\}_{r\in\mathbbm{N}}
from Assumption
6
. We further find that the semigroup satisfies the bound

(42)

\norm{\cP_t}_{W^{\textbf{k}_{r},1}\to W^{\textbf{k}_{r},1}}\leq e^{\omega_{\textbf{k}_{r}}t}\,\quad\forall t\geq 0\,.

In the special case
\textbf{k}=0
, the semigroup is contractive and trace-preserving.

Note that we can extend the above semigroups to the convex hull of
\{\textbf{k}_{r}\}_{r\in\mathbbm{N}}\cup\{0\}
using a generalisation of the interpolation lemma for single mode semigroups (q.v. Lemma
E.4
).

Lastly, we can also generalize the generation theorem for evolution systems modifying the assumptions accordingly.

Assumption 7

The operator
(\mathcal{L}_{s},\mathcal{T}_{f})
has
\operatorname{GKSL}
form, i.e. for
x\in\mathcal{T}_{f}
and
s\in[0,\infty)

(43)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{s}:\mathcal{T}_{f}\to\mathcal{T}_{f}\quad x\mapsto\mathcal{L}_{s}(x)

\displaystyle=-i[H(s),x]+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{K}L_{j}(s)xL_{j}^{\dagger}(s)-\frac{1}{2}\{L_{j}^{\dagger}(s)L_{j}(s),x\}

\displaystyle\coloneqq G(s)x+xG^{\dagger}(s)+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{K}L_{j}(s)xL_{j}^{\dagger}(s)\,,

where
K\in\mathbb{N}
,
G(s)=-iH(s)-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{K}L_{j}^{\dagger}(s)L_{j}(s)
, and
H(s)\coloneqq p_{H(s)}(a_{1},a^{\dagger}_{1},\ldots,a_{m},a^{\dagger}_{m})
,
L_{j}(s)\coloneqq p_{j,s}(a_{1},a^{\dagger}_{1},\ldots,a_{m},a^{\dagger}_{m})
are polynomials of the creation and annihilation operators with time-dependent, continuous coefficients. Again,
d_{H}\coloneqq\sup_{s\geq 0}\,\deg(p_{H(s)})<\infty
,
d_{j}\coloneqq\sup_{s\geq 0}\deg(p_{j,s})<\infty
, and
d\coloneqq\max\{d_{1},...,d_{K},d_{H}\}
.

The second assumption in the time-dependent case generalizes to the following:

Assumption 8

There is a divergent sequence
\{\textbf{k}_{r}\}_{r\in\mathbbm{N}}\subset\mathbbm{R}_{+}^{m}
, meaning
\lim\limits_{r\to\infty}\min\limits_{j=1,\ldots,m}k_{j,r}=\infty
, s.t. for every
r\in\mathbbm{N}
, there exist
\omega_{\textbf{k}_{r}}\geq 0
such that for all
s\in\mathbbm{R}_{+}
and
x\in\mathcal{T}_{f}
positive semi-definite,

(44)

\tr[\mathcal{L}_{s}(x)(N+\mathbbm{1})^{\textbf{k}_{r}/2}]\leq\omega_{\textbf{k}_{r}}\tr[x(N+\mathbbm{1})^{\textbf{k}_{r}/2}]\,.

Note that the coefficients
\omega_{\textbf{k}_{r}}
are independent of
s
.

Under the above assumptions we can state the generation theorem for multi-mode evolution systems as follows:

Theorem 3.10

(Generation of multi-mode bosonic evolution systems)

Let
(\mathcal{L}_{s},\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{L}_{s}))_{s\in[0,\infty)}
be a family of operators that fulfills Assumption
7
and Assumption
8
. Then

(\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{s},\mathcal{D}(\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{s}))_{s\in\mathbbm{R}_{+}}
gives rise to a unique evolution system
(\mathcal{P}_{t,s})_{0\leq s\leq t}
on
W^{\textbf{k}_{r},1}
for all
r\in\mathbbm{N}
with the following properties: for
\textbf{k}_{r^{\prime}}
with
\min\limits_{j=1,\ldots,m}|k_{j,r}-k_{j,r^{\prime}}|\geq d

1.

\mathcal{P}_{t,s}(W^{\textbf{k}_{r^{\prime}},1})\subseteq W^{\textbf{k}_{r^{\prime}},1}
for all
0\leq s\leq t
;

2.

For any
x\in W^{\textbf{k}_{r^{\prime}},1}
, the family
(\mathcal{P}_{t,s}(x))_{0\leq s\leq t}
is the unique solution to the initial value problem

(45)

\frac{d}{dt}x(t)=\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{t}(x(t))\qquad t\in[s,\infty),\;x(s)=x\,.

For
\textbf{k}=0
as a special case, we get that the evolution system is contractive and trace-preserving.

4
Examples of Sobolev preserving semigroups

In this section, we consider two classes of examples of practical relevance in quantum information processing for which Assumption
1
(or
3
,
5
,
7
) trivially holds and derive Assumption
2
(or
4
,
6
,
8
). Particular care will be given to finding time-independent upper bounds on the
W^{k,1}\to W^{k,1}
norm of the semigroup. For this, the overall strategy is as follows: given the generator
(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{T}_{f})
, we prove that there are coefficients
\mu_{k_{r}}\geq 0,c_{k_{r}}>0
for a divergent sequence
\{k_{r}\}_{r\in\mathbbm{N}}
such that for all state
\rho\in\mathcal{T}_{f}

(46)

\displaystyle\tr[\mathcal{L}(\rho)({N}+\mathbbm{1})^{k_{r}/2}]

\displaystyle\leq-c_{k_{r}}\tr[\rho({N}+\mathbbm{1})^{k_{r}/2}]+\mu_{k_{r}}

\displaystyle\leq(\mu_{k_{r}}-c_{k_{r}})\tr[\rho({N}+\mathbbm{1})^{k_{r}/2}]\,,

where we have used
\tr[\rho({N}+\mathbbm{1})^{k_{r}/2}]\geq\tr[\rho]=1
in the second inequality.
Then, Theorem
3.1
can be applied, which shows that for all
k\in\mathbbm{R}_{+}
, the closure of
(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{T}_{f})
generates a positivity preserving
C_{0}
-semigroup
(\mathcal{P}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
on
W^{k,1}
. In the case
k\in\{k_{r}\}_{r\in\mathbbm{N}}
:

(47)

\|\mathcal{P}_{t}(x)\|_{W^{k,1}}\leq e^{|\mu_{k}-c_{k}|\,t}\|x\|_{W^{k,1}}\,.

for all
x\in W^{k,1}
. The bounds for the intermediate values of
k
can be obtained using Lemma
E.4
. One can strengthen the above bounds using
Equation

46
as follows:

Proposition 4.1

Let
(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{T}_{f})
be an operator satisfying Assumption
1
and
Equation

46
. Then, for all
k\in\mathbbm{N}
, the closure of
(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{T}_{f})
generates a positivity preserving
C_{0}
-semigroup
(\mathcal{P}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
on
W^{k,1}
. For all
r\in\mathbbm{N}
and all states
\rho\in W^{k,1}
,

\|\mathcal{P}_{t}(\rho)\|_{W^{k_{r},1}}\leq\max\left\{\|\rho\|_{W^{k_{r},1}},\,\frac{\mu_{k_{r}}}{c_{k_{r}}}\right\}\,.

For a general
k\in\mathbbm{R}_{+}
and
x\in W^{k,1}
one obtains

(48)

\|\mathcal{P}_{t}(x)\|_{W^{k_{r},1}}\leq\gamma_{k}\|x\|_{W^{k_{r},1}}\,,

where
\gamma_{k}=\max\{1,\frac{\mu_{k}}{c_{k}}\}
for
k\in\{k_{r}\}_{r\in\mathbbm{N}}
and an interpolated time-independent constant in all other cases. Note that for
k>0
and
\rho\in W^{k,1}
there exists a sequence
\{t_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbbm{N}}
, such that

\lim_{t_{n}\rightarrow\infty}\mathcal{P}_{t_{n}}(\rho)=\overline{\rho}

for
\overline{\rho}\in W^{k,1}
. Similar conclusions hold in multi-mode as well as time-dependent settings.

Proof.

By assumption, Theorem
3.1
shows that the closure of
(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{T}_{f})
defines a positivity preserving, quasi-contractive semigroup
(\mathcal{P}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
. Moreover, for
k\in\{k_{r}\}_{r\in\mathbbm{N}}
,
\rho(t)\coloneqq\mathcal{P}_{t}(\rho)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\rho(t)\|_{W^{k,1}}

\displaystyle=\tr[\mathcal{L}(\rho(t))(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2}]

\displaystyle\leq-c_{k}\tr[\rho(t)({N}+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2}]+\mu_{k}

\displaystyle=-c_{k}\|\rho\|_{W^{k,1}}+\mu_{k}\,.

Thus, for
\|\rho(t)\|_{W^{k,1}}\geq\frac{\mu_{k}}{c_{k}}
, we have
\frac{d}{dt}\|\rho(t)\|_{W^{k,1}}\leq 0
, which concludes the bound. Using the positivity preserving property of the semigroup and that
\norm{\cdot}_{1}\leq\norm{\cdot}_{W^{k,1}}
one can lift the bound to
Equation

48
for general
x\in W^{k,1}
and Theorem
2.14
allows us to conclude

\|\mathcal{P}_{t}(x)\|_{W^{k_{r},1}}\leq\gamma_{k}\|x\|_{W^{k_{r},1}}

extend to all
k\in\mathbbm{R}_{+}
. Finally, for every
k>0
, every sequence
n\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{t_{n}}(\rho)
is uniformly bounded in
W^{k,1}
so that the compact embedding shows that there exists a converging subsequence in
W^{k-\varepsilon,1}
for
\varepsilon
suitably chosen, which is also converging in
W^{k,1}
. This finishes the proof.
∎

To achieve the inequality stated in
Equation

46
, we will make heavy use of the following simple commutation relations: given a real-valued function
f:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{R}
,

(49)

\displaystyle af({N}+j\mathbbm{1})=f({N}+(j+1)\mathbbm{1})a,

\displaystyle a^{\dagger}\,1_{>j}f({N}-j\mathbbm{1})=f({N}-(j+1)\mathbbm{1})a^{\dagger}\,1_{>j}\,,

\displaystyle f({N}-j\mathbbm{1})a\,1_{>j}=af({N}-(j+1)\mathbbm{1})1_{>j},

\displaystyle f({N}+j\mathbbm{1})a^{\dagger}=a^{\dagger}f({N}+(j+1)\mathbbm{1})\,,

where the operators above are defined e.g. on
\mathcal{H}_{f}
. We also use the canonical commutation relation to write
(a^{\dagger})^{l}a^{l}
as a function of
N
(see Lemma
B.2
):

\displaystyle(a^{\dagger})^{l}a^{l}=(N-(l-1)\mathbbm{1})(N-(l-2)\mathbbm{1})\cdots(N-\mathbbm{1})N

\displaystyle a^{l}(a^{\dagger})^{l}=(N+\mathbbm{1})(N+2\mathbbm{1})\cdots(N+(l-1)\mathbbm{1})(N+l\mathbbm{1})\,.

In the following, we adopt the notations:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}[L]\coloneqq L(\cdot)L^{\dagger}-\frac{1}{2}\,\{L^{\dagger}L,\,\cdot\}\,\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\mathcal{H}[H]\coloneqq-i[H,\cdot]\,.

Although this notation collides with the one for the Hilbert space, the meaning can always be deduced from context.

4.1
Quantum Ornstein Uhlenbeck semigroup

We start with the generator of the quantum Ornstein Uhlenbeck semigroup
[
18
,
13
]
defined by

(50)

\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{qOU}}=\lambda^{2}\mathcal{L}[a]+\mu^{2}\mathcal{L}[a^{\dagger}]

for
\mu,\lambda\geq 0
. Given an suitably domain
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{qOU}})
, the operator
(\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\operatorname{qOU}},\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{qOU}}))

is known to generate a quantum dynamical semigroup
(\mathcal{P}_{t}^{\operatorname{qOU}})_{t\geq 0}
. Here, we further show that the quantum Ornstein Uhlenbeck semigroup defines a semigroup on all
W^{k,1}
. This is the topic of the following lemma:

Lemma 4.2

Let
(\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{qOU}},\mathcal{T}_{f})
be the generator of the quantum Ornstein Uhlenbeck semigroup and
k\in\mathbbm{N}
. Then, there exist constants
\mu_{k}
explicated in (
52
) such that, for all states
\rho\in\mathcal{T}_{f}
,

\begin{aligned} \tr[\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{qOU}}(\rho)(N+\mathbbm{1})^{\frac{k}{2}}]\leq\begin{cases}\frac{k}{4}(\mu^{2}-\lambda^{2})\tr[\rho(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2}\big]+\mu_{k}&\lambda>\mu\\
\frac{k}{2}(2\mu^{2}+k)\tr[\rho(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2}\big]&\lambda\leq\mu\end{cases}\end{aligned}\,.

Therefore, the semigroup
e^{t\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{qOU}}}
is a Sobolev and positivity preserving quantum Markov semigroup satisfying for all states
\rho\in W^{k,1}

\displaystyle\|e^{t\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{qOU}}}(\rho)\|_{W^{k,1}}\leq\begin{cases}\max\left\{\|\rho\|_{W^{k,1}},\,\frac{4\mu_{k}}{k(\mu^{2}-\lambda^{2})}\right\}&\lambda>\mu\\
e^{t\frac{k}{2}(2\mu^{2}+k)}\|\rho\|_{W^{k,1}}&\lambda\leq\mu\end{cases}\,.

Proof.

We consider
\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{qOU}}^{\dagger}(f(N))
where
f(x)=(x+1)^{k/2}1_{x\geq-1}
. By
Equation

49
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{qOU}}^{\dagger}(f(N))

\displaystyle=\lambda^{2}N(f(N-\mathbbm{1})-f(N))+\mu^{2}(N+\mathbbm{1})(f(N+\mathbbm{1})-f(N))\,.

Note that the case
k=0
follows from the GKLS form and
k=2
is by definition of
f
trivially given by
(\mu^{2}-\lambda^{2})N+\mathbbm{1}
.
Next, we define an auxiliary function which will also prove useful in the following proofs:

(51)

g_{l}(x)=\begin{cases}f(x)-f(x-l)&x\geq l;\\
f(x)&l>x\geq 0;\\
0&0>x\,.\end{cases}

It allows us to redefine
\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{qOU}}^{\dagger}(f(N))
by

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{qOU}}^{\dagger}(f(N))

\displaystyle=-\lambda^{2}Ng_{1}(N)+\mu^{2}(N+\mathbbm{1})g_{1}(N+\mathbbm{1})\,.

Then, applying Lemma
C.2
to the spectral decomposition of the polynomial in the number operator above, we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{qOU}}^{\dagger}(f(N))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k}{2}(\mu^{2}-\lambda^{2})(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2}+\lambda^{2}\frac{k}{2}(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2-1}+1_{k\geq 3}(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2-2}\frac{k^{2}}{8}+\mu^{2}\frac{2-k}{2}\outerproduct{0}{0}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k}{2}(\mu^{2}-\lambda^{2})(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2}+\frac{k}{2}\left(\lambda^{2}+\mu^{2}+{k}\right)(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2-1}

where we separated the vacuum state from the rest of the decomposition. Note that this bound can also be used when
k=1
since
(N+\mathbbm{1})^{-1/2}
is then bounded by
1
. Therefore, we assume
k\geq 3
in the following and start with the case
\lambda>\mu
so that the leading order is negative. Then, we use half of the latter to bound the other terms by a constant. This is done by the following classical optimization

\sup_{x\geq 0}\left(-x^{\nu}+cx^{\nu-1}\right)=c^{\nu}\left(\frac{(\nu-1)^{\nu-1}}{\nu^{\nu}}\right)

for
\nu\geq 1
and
c\geq 0
defined as

c=2\frac{\lambda^{2}+\mu^{2}+k}{\lambda^{2}-\mu^{2}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\nu=\frac{k}{2}\,.

Then,

(52)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{qOU}}^{\dagger}(f(N))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k}{4}(\mu^{2}-\lambda^{2})(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2}+c^{\nu}\left(\frac{(\nu-1)^{\nu-1}}{\nu^{\nu}}\right)

\displaystyle=:\frac{k}{4}(\mu^{2}-\lambda^{2})(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2}+\mu_{k}^{\lambda>\mu}

The second case is
\lambda\leq\mu
, which can be easily upper bounded by

\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{qOU}}^{\dagger}(f(N))\leq\frac{k}{2}(2\mu^{2}+k)(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2}\end{aligned}\,.

This completes the proof of the statement by Theorem
3.1
and Proposition
4.1
.
∎

4.2
Photon-dissipation and CAT qubits

Next, we consider a family of Lindbladians that has been recently studied in the setting of error correction with continuous variable quantum systems. For an introduction to the field, we refer the interested reader to the following lecture notes
[
57
,
32
]
. The abstract idea here is that the code-space is continuously protected by a dissipative evolution, i.e. an evolution which is exponentially converging for
t\rightarrow\infty
to an invariant subspace — the code-space. This behavior is achieved through the so-called
l
-photon dissipation generated for
\kappa>0
and
\alpha\in\mathbbm{C}
by

(53)

\kappa\mathcal{L}[a^{l}-\alpha^{l}]\,,

where we sometimes omit the identity so that
\alpha^{l}\coloneqq\alpha^{l}\mathbbm{1}
in what follows. The invariant subspace (code-space) to which the evolution is exponentially converging
[
5
]
is defined by

(54)

\mathcal{C}_{l}\coloneqq\textrm{span}\left\{\outerproduct{\alpha_1}{\alpha_2}\,:\,\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in\left\{\alpha e^{\frac{i2\pi j}{l}}\,|\,j\in\{0,...,l-1\}\right\}\right\}\,,

where
\ket{\alpha}
denotes the coherent state

\ket{\alpha}=e^{-\frac{|\alpha|^{2}}{2}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\alpha^{n}}{\sqrt{n!}}\ket{n}\,.

and satisfies
a\ket{\alpha}=\alpha\ket{\alpha}
by definition.

Besides the
l
-photon dissipation, we consider the CAT qubit error correction protocol introduced in
[
33
]
associated with the
2
-photon dissipation and code-space
\mathcal{C}_{2}
and with corresponding universal gate-set generated by the following generators: for some parameters
T,\kappa,\varepsilon>0
,

Identity-gate:

(55)

\kappa\mathcal{L}[a^{2}-\alpha^{2}]

Z(\theta)
-gate:

(56)

\kappa\mathcal{L}[a^{2}-\alpha^{2}]+\varepsilon\mathcal{H}[a+a^{\dagger}]

X
-gate:

(57)

\kappa\mathcal{L}[a^{2}-e^{2i\pi t/T}\alpha^{2}]

\operatorname{CNOT}
-gate:

(58)

\kappa\mathcal{L}[a^{2}-\alpha^{2}]+\varepsilon\mathcal{L}[b^{2}-\alpha^{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}(1-e^{2i\pi t/T})(a-\alpha)]

Toffoli-gate:

(59)

\kappa\mathcal{L}[a^{2}-\alpha^{2}]+\kappa\mathcal{L}[b^{2}-\alpha^{2}]+\varepsilon\mathcal{L}[c^{2}-\alpha^{2}+\frac{1}{4}(1-e^{2i\pi t/T})(ab-\alpha(a+b)+\alpha^{2})]\,.

Note that the CNOT gate operates on two modes, while the Toffoli gate acts on three modes, with the annihilation and creation operators on the second mode denoted by
b
and
b^{\dagger}
, and on the third mode by
c
and
c^{\dagger}
. Furthermore, from a mathematical standpoint, the implementation remains somewhat unclear, as the sum gates are constructed using an adiabatic limit, for which, to our knowledge, rigorous error bounds have yet to be established.

In the following, we prove that the above operators generate Sobolev-preserving quantum dynamical semigroups, with the exception of the Toffoli gate. Due to its more complicated structure, we leave the analysis of the latter to future work. We start by proving that the
l
-photon dissipation satisfies
Equation

46
, and therefore that it generates a Sobolev preserving semigroup by Proposition
4.1
.

Lemma 4.3

(
l
-photon dissipation)

For any
k\geq 1
,
l\geq 2
,
\alpha\in\mathbb{C}
and any state
\rho\in\mathcal{T}_{f}
,

\displaystyle\tr\big[\cL[a^l-\alpha^l ](\rho)(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2}\big]

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{l}{2}\tr[\rho\,(N+\mathbbm{1})^{\nu}\big]+\frac{l}{2}\mu_{k}^{(l)}\leq-\frac{l}{2}\tr[\rho\,(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2}\big]+\frac{l}{2}\mu_{k}^{(l)}\,,

where
\mu_{k}^{(l)}=\Delta_{l}^{\nu}\left(\frac{(\nu-1)^{\nu-1}}{\nu^{\nu}}\right)
with
\nu=l+\frac{k}{2}-1
and
\Delta_{l}=(l+1)l+2|\alpha|^{l}kl^{k/2-1}\sqrt{l!}
. Therefore,
\mathcal{L}_{l}\coloneqq\mathcal{L}[a^{l}-\alpha^{l}]
generates a Sobolev and positivity preserving quantum Markov semigroup satisfying for all states
\rho\in W^{k,1}

\|e^{t\mathcal{L}_{l}}(\rho)\|_{W^{k,1}}\leq\max\Big\{\|\rho\|_{W^{k,1}},\mu_{k}^{(l)}\Big\}\,.

Proof.

By
Equation

49
, we have for
f(x)=(x+1)^{k/2}1_{x\geq-1}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}[a_{1}^{l}-\alpha^{l}]^{\dagger}(f(N))

\displaystyle=(a^{\dagger})^{l}f(N)a^{l}-\frac{1}{2}\Big((a^{\dagger})^{l}a^{l}f(N)+f(N)(a^{\dagger})^{l}a^{l}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}(\overline{\alpha}^{l}a^{l}f(N)-\overline{\alpha}^{l}f(N)a^{l}+\alpha^{l}f(N)(a^{\dagger})^{l}-\alpha^{l}(a^{\dagger})^{l}f(N))

\displaystyle=(a^{\dagger})^{l}a^{l}\Big(f(N-l\mathbbm{1})-f(N)\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\left[\overline{\alpha}^{l}a^{l}\Big(f(N)-f(N-l\mathbbm{1})\Big)+\alpha^{l}\Big(f(N)-f(N-l\mathbbm{1})\Big)(a^{\dagger})^{l}\right]\,.

In what follows, we use the function defined in
Equation

91

g_{l}(x)=\begin{cases}f(x)-f(x-l)&x\geq l;\\
f(x)&l>x\geq 0;\\
0&0>x\,.\end{cases}

Using the canonical commutation relation to write
(a^{\dagger})^{l}a^{l}
as a function of
N
(cf. Lemma
B.2
) and with help of the notation

(60)

N_{k}[r:j]\coloneqq(N_{k}+r\mathbbm{1})\cdots(N_{k}+j\mathbbm{1})

with the convention
N_{k}[r:j]=\mathbbm{1}
whenever
r>j
, we thus have that

\displaystyle\tr[\rho\,\mathcal{L}[a^{l}-\alpha^{l}]^{\dagger}(f(N))\big]

\displaystyle=-\tr[\rho\,N[-l+1:0]g_{l}(N)\big]+\frac{1}{2}\tr[\rho\,(\overline{\alpha}^{l}a^{l}g_{l}(N)+\alpha^{l}g_{l}(N)(a^{\dagger})^{l})\big]\,,

Since
g_{l}
is positive and increasing, the last term above can be upper bounded by Lemma
B.3
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\tr[\rho(\overline{\alpha}^{l}a^{l}g_{l}(N)+{\alpha}^{l}g_{l}(N)(a^{\dagger})^{l})\big]

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\leq}\,{|\alpha|^{l}}\tr[\rho\,g_{l}(N+l\mathbbm{1})\sqrt{N[1:l]}]

\displaystyle\overset{(1)}{\leq}|\alpha|^{l}\frac{kl^{k/2}}{2}\,\tr[\rho\,(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2-1}\sqrt{N[1:l]}]

\displaystyle\leq|\alpha|^{l}kl^{k/2}\sqrt{l!}\,\tr[\rho\,(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2-1+\frac{l}{2}}]\,.

In
(1)
above, we used Lemma
C.2
for the bound

\displaystyle g_{l}(N+l\mathbbm{1})\leq\frac{kl}{2}\,(N+l\mathbbm{1})^{k/2-1}\leq\frac{kl^{k/2}}{2}\,(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2-1}\,.

Therefore, we have proven that

\displaystyle\tr[\rho\mathcal{L}[a^{l}-\alpha^{l}]^{\dagger}(f(N))\big]

\displaystyle\leq-\tr[\rho\,N[-l+1:0]\,g_{l}(N)\big]

\displaystyle+|\alpha|^{l}kl^{k/2}\sqrt{l!}\,\tr[\rho\,(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2-1+\frac{l}{2}}]\,.

Next, we upper bound the first term above

\displaystyle\tr[\rho\,N[-l+1:0]\,g_{l}(N)\big]

\displaystyle\overset{(3)}{\geq}l\tr\big[\rho\,N[-l+1:0](N+\1)^{k/2-1}\big]

\displaystyle\overset{(4)}{\geq}l\,\tr[\rho\,(N+\mathbbm{1})^{l+k/2-1}\,\big]-\frac{(l+1)l^{2}}{2}\tr[\rho\,(N+\mathbbm{1})^{l+k/2-2}\big]\,.

In
(3)
, we used Lemma
C.2
below with the fact that
N[-l+1:0]
is supported on the Fock states
|n\rangle
with
n\geq l-1
;
in
(4)
we used that

\displaystyle N[-l+1:0]

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\geq 0}\,(n-l+1)\dots n\,\outerproduct{n}{n}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\geq l}\,(n-l+1)\dots n\,\outerproduct{n}{n}

\displaystyle\overset{(5)}{\geq}\sum_{n\geq l}\left((n+1)^{l}-\frac{(l+1)l}{2}(n+1)^{l-1}\right)\outerproduct{n}{n}

\displaystyle\geq(N+\mathbbm{1})^{l}-\frac{(l+1)l}{2}\,(N+\mathbbm{1})^{l-1}\,,

where
(5)
comes from Lemma
C.3
below, whereas the last inequality follows from the fact that
l\geq 2
. To sum up, we showed that

(61)

\displaystyle\tr\big[\cL[a^l-\alpha^l](\rho)(f(N))\big]

\displaystyle\leq-l\tr[\rho\,(N+\mathbbm{1})^{l+k/2-1}\,\big]+\frac{(l+1)l^{2}}{2}\tr[\rho\,(N+\mathbbm{1})^{l+k/2-2}\big]

\displaystyle+|\alpha|^{l}kl^{k/2}\sqrt{l!}\,\tr[\rho\,(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2-1+\frac{l}{2}}]

\displaystyle\leq-l\tr[\rho\,(N+\mathbbm{1})^{l+k/2-1}\,\big]

\displaystyle+\frac{l}{2}\biggl(\underbrace{{(l+1)l}+2|\alpha|^{l}kl^{k/2-1}\sqrt{l!}}_{\eqqcolon\Delta_{l}}\biggr)\,\tr[\rho\,(N+\mathbbm{1})^{l+k/2-2}\big]

where we used again that
l\geq 2
in the last inequality. Half of the leading order term can be used to control the second term by a constant. For that, we use the spectral decomposition of the operator
N
so that the above problem can be reduced to the following simple optimization:

(62)

\sup_{x\geq 0}\left(-x^{\nu}+\Delta_{l}x^{\nu-1}\right)=\Delta_{l}^{\nu}\left(\frac{(\nu-1)^{\nu-1}}{\nu^{\nu}}\right)

for
\nu\geq 1
defined as

\nu=l+\frac{k}{2}-1\,.

The result follows after invoking Proposition
4.1
.
∎

Remark 7
.

The single-mode bound proved above can be generalized to the multi-mode setting, with generated given for some
\alpha_{j}\in\mathbb{C}
,
j\in[m]
, by

\mathcal{L}_{l}^{(m)}\coloneqq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{L}[a_{j}^{l}-\alpha_{j}^{l}]\,.

Since all the bounds used in the proof of Lemma
4.3
were derived at the operator level, we directly get for
\textbf{k}\in\mathbbm{N}^{m}

\displaystyle\tr[\mathcal{L}_{l}^{(m)}(\rho)(N+\mathbbm{1})^{\textbf{k}/2}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{m}-\frac{l}{2}\tr[\rho\,(N_{i}+\mathbbm{1})^{l-1}(N+\mathbbm{1})^{\textbf{k}/2}\big]+\mu^{(l)}_{k_{i}}\tr[\rho\,\prod_{j\neq i}(N_{j}+\mathbbm{1})^{k_{j}/2}\big]

For later references, we single out the case
l=2
.

Corollary 4.4

(
2
-photon dissipation)

For any integers
k\geq 1
,
\alpha\in\mathbb{C}
and any state
\rho\in\mathcal{T}_{f}
,

\displaystyle\tr\big[\cL[a^2-\alpha^2](\rho)(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2}\big]

\displaystyle\leq-\tr[\rho\,(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2}\big]+\mu^{(2)}_{k}

where
\mu^{(2)}_{k}=(\Delta^{(2)}_{k})^{\nu}\left(\frac{(\nu-1)^{\nu-1}}{\nu^{\nu}}\right)
with
\nu=\frac{k}{2}+1
and
\Delta_{2}=6+2\sqrt{2!}|\alpha|^{2}k2^{k/2-1}
. Therefore,
\mathcal{L}_{2}\coloneqq\mathcal{L}[a^{2}-\alpha^{2}]
generates a Sobolev and positivity preserving quantum Markov semigroup which satisfies for all states
\rho
in
W^{k,1}

\|e^{t\mathcal{L}_{2}}(\rho)\|_{W^{k,1}}\leq\max\left\{\|\rho\|_{W^{k,1}},\mu^{(2)}_{k}\right\}\,.

From the above bounds, we directly get the property of Sobolev preservation for the
X
-gate:

Corollary 4.5

(
X
-gate)

For any
T>0
,
\alpha\in\mathbb{C}
,
k\in\mathbbm{N}
and all states
\rho\in\mathcal{T}_{f}
,

\tr[\cL[a^2-e^{2i\pi t/T}\alpha^2](\rho)(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2}]\leq-\tr[\rho(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2}]+\mu_{k}^{(2)}\,,

where
\mu_{k}^{(2)}
is defined in Lemma
4.4
. Therefore,
\mathcal{L}[a^{2}-e^{2i\pi t/T}\alpha^{2}]
generates a Sobolev and positivity preserving quantum evolution system
\mathcal{P}_{t,t_{0}}
which satisfies for all states
\rho\in W^{k,1}

\|\mathcal{P}_{t,t_{0}}(\rho)\|_{W^{k,1}}\leq\max\left\{\|\rho\|_{W^{k,1}},\mu_{k}^{(2)}\right\}\,.

Proof.

The statement directly follows from Lemma
4.4
and
|e^{2i\pi t/T}\alpha^{2}|=|\alpha^{2}|

∎

Since
Equation

46
, which implies Assumption
2
(or
8
), is linear in the supposed generators and the leading order found in
Equation

61
has the ability to suppress smaller terms, certain Hamiltonians can be regularized in the sense of
Equation

46
by adding an
l
-photon dissipation. Especially, we consider a Hamiltonian of degree
d_{H}=2(l-1)
with the structure: for
\lambda_{i,j}\in\mathbb{C}
with
\max_{i,j}|\lambda_{i,j}|=\Lambda
,

(63)

H=p(a,a^{\dagger})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\leq j\\
i+j\leq d_{H}\end{subarray}}\lambda_{i,j}a^{i}(a^{\dagger})^{j}+\overline{\lambda_{i,j}}a^{j}(a^{\dagger})^{i}\,.

Note that any monomial in
a,a^{\dagger}
of degree at most
d_{H}
can be achieved from the representation above thanks to the CCR.

Lemma 4.6

Let
\mathcal{L}_{l}\coloneqq\mathcal{L}[a^{l}-\alpha^{l}]
,
\alpha\in\mathbb{C}
, be the
l
-photon dissipation and
H
as in (
63
). Then, for all states
\rho\in\mathcal{T}_{f}

(64)

\displaystyle\tr[(\mathcal{L}_{l}+\mathcal{H}[H])(\rho)(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2}]

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{l}{2}\,\tr[\rho(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2}]+\frac{l}{2}\mu_{k}\,.

for
\mu_{k},\nu\geq 1
defined by

\mu_{k}=c^{\nu}\left(\frac{(\nu-1)^{\nu-1}}{\nu^{\nu}}\right)\quad\text{with}\quad c={(l+1)l}+2|\alpha|^{l}kl^{k/2-1}\sqrt{l!}+\Lambda(2l)^{k/2}\sqrt{(2l)!}\,,\quad\nu=l+\frac{k}{2}-1\,.

Therefore,
\mathcal{L}_{l}+\mathcal{H}[H]
generates a Sobolev and positivity preserving quantum Markov semigroup which satisfies for all states
\rho\in W^{k,1}

(65)

\|e^{t(\mathcal{L}_{l}+\mathcal{H}[H])}(\rho)\|_{W^{k,1}}\leq\max\Big\{\|\rho\|_{W^{k,1}},\mu_{k}\Big\}\,.

Proof.

We reuse the bound given in
Equation

61
:

\displaystyle\tr[\rho\mathcal{L}_{l}^{\dagger}(f(N))]\leq-l\tr[\rho\,(N+\mathbbm{1})^{l+k/2-1}\,\big]+\frac{l}{2}\Delta_{l}\,\tr[\rho\,(N+\mathbbm{1})^{l+k/2-2}g_{l}(N)\big]\,,

where
f(x)=(x+1)^{k/2}1_{x\geq-1}
and
\Delta_{l}={(l+1)l}+2|\alpha|^{l}kl^{k/2-1}\sqrt{l!}
. To upper bound

\displaystyle\tr[\cH[H](\rho)(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2}]

\displaystyle=i\tr[\rho[(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2},H]]\,,

we define
g_{u}
similarly to
Equation

51
by

g_{u}(x)=\begin{cases}f(x)-f(x-u)&x\geq u-1;\\
f(x)&u-1>x\geq 0;\\
0&0>x\,.\end{cases}

For
d_{H}=0
the bound is trivial, so we assume
d_{H}\geq 1
. Then, we compute

\displaystyle i[

\displaystyle f(N),H]

\displaystyle=i\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq j<i\\
0<i+j\leq d_{H}\end{subarray}}f(N)(\lambda_{i,j}(a^{\dagger})^{i}a^{j}+\overline{\lambda_{i,j}}(a^{\dagger})^{j}a^{i})-(\lambda_{i,j}(a^{\dagger})^{i}a^{j}+\overline{\lambda_{i,j}}(a^{\dagger})^{j}a^{i})f(N)

\displaystyle=i\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq j<i\\
0<i+j\leq d_{H}\end{subarray}}\lambda_{i,j}f(N)N[-i+1:-i+j](a^{\dagger})^{i-j}+\overline{\lambda_{i,j}}a^{i-j}f(N-i+j)N[-i+1:-i+j]

\displaystyle-\lambda_{i,j}N[-i+1:-i+j]f(N-i+j)(a^{\dagger})^{i-j}-\overline{\lambda_{i,j}}a^{i-j}N[-i+1:-i+j]f(N)

\displaystyle=i\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<r\leq i\\
0<2i-r\leq d_{H}\end{subarray}}-\overline{\lambda_{i,i-r}}a^{r}N[-i+1:-r]g_{r}(N)+\lambda_{i,i-r}g_{r}(N)N[-i+1:-r](a^{\dagger})^{r}

\displaystyle\overset{(1)}{\leq}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<r\leq i\\
0<2i-r\leq d_{H}\end{subarray}}2|\lambda_{i,i-r}|\sqrt{(N+\mathbbm{1})\cdots(N+r\mathbbm{1})}g_{r}(N+r\mathbbm{1})N[r-i+1:0]

\displaystyle\overset{(2)}{\leq}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<r\leq i\\
0<2i-r\leq d_{H}\end{subarray}}2\sqrt{r!}|\lambda_{i,i-r}|g_{r}(N+r\mathbbm{1})(N+\mathbbm{1})^{i-r/2}

\displaystyle i\tr[[H,\rho](N+\1)^{k/2}]

\displaystyle\leq 2\Lambda\sum_{i=1}^{d_{H}}\sum_{r=1}^{i}\sqrt{r!}\tr[\rho g_{r}(N+r)(N+\mathbbm{1})^{i-r/2}]

\displaystyle\overset{(3)}{\leq}2\Lambda\sum_{i=1}^{d_{H}}\sum_{r=1}^{i}\sqrt{r!}r^{k/2-1}\tr[\rho(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2+i-r/2-1}]

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda(d_{H}+1)d_{H}\sqrt{d_{H}!}d_{H}^{k/2-1}\tr[\rho(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2+d_{H}/2-1}]\,,

where we used Lemma
C.2
in
(3)
. As the above function is monotone in
d_{H}
we can w.l.o.g assume
d_{H}=2(l-1)
and conclude

\displaystyle\tr[(\mathcal{L}_{l}+\mathcal{H}[H])(f(N))]

\displaystyle\leq-l\tr[\rho\,(N+\mathbbm{1})^{l+k/2-1}\,\big]

\displaystyle+\frac{l}{2}\left(\Delta_{l}+\Lambda(2l)^{k/2}\sqrt{(2l)!}\right)\tr[\rho(N)(N+\mathbbm{1})^{l+k/2-2}]\,.

The same optimization as in
Equation

62
provides inequality (
64
). Inequality (
65
) follows after invoking Proposition
4.1
.
∎

The case
l=2
deals with the sum of a
2
-photon dissipation with the displacement operator
i[a+a^{\dagger},\cdot]
used in the construction of the
Z(\theta)
-gate
[
50
]
. In this specific case, one can improve the error bound in the following way:

Lemma 4.7

(
Z(\theta)
-gate)

For any state
\rho\in\mathcal{T}_{f}
,
\alpha\in\mathbb{C}
,
\varepsilon>0
and
k\in\mathbbm{N}

\tr[(\varepsilon\mathcal{H}[a+a^{\dagger}]+\mathcal{L}[a^{2}+\alpha^{2}])(\rho)(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2}]\leq-\,\tr[\rho\,(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2}\big]+\mu_{k}\,.

where
\mu_{k}\geq 0
is defined by

\mu_{k}=(\Delta_{2}+\varepsilon 4k)^{\nu}\left(\frac{(\nu-1)^{\nu-1}}{\nu^{\nu}}\right)\qquad\text{with}\qquad\nu=\frac{k}{2}+1\,.

Therefore,
\varepsilon\mathcal{H}[a+a^{\dagger}]+\mathcal{L}[a^{2}+\alpha^{2}]
generates a Sobolev and positivity preserving quantum Markov semigroup which satisfies for all states
\rho\in W^{k,1}

(66)

\|e^{t(\varepsilon\mathcal{H}[a+a^{\dagger}]+\mathcal{L}[a^{2}+\alpha^{2}])}(\rho)\|_{W^{k,1}}\leq\max\Big\{\|\rho\|_{W^{k,1}},\mu_{k}\Big\}\,.

Proof.

By
Equation

61
in Lemma
4.3
,

\displaystyle\tr\big[\cL[a^2-\alpha^2](\rho)(f(N))\big]

\displaystyle\leq-2\tr[\rho\,(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2+1}\,\big]

\displaystyle+\biggl(\underbrace{6+2|\alpha|^{2}k2^{k/2-1}\sqrt{2}}_{\eqqcolon\Delta_{2}}\biggr)\,\tr[\rho\,(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2}\big]

where
f(x)=(x+1)^{k/2}1_{x\geq-1}
. Next, by
Equation

49
, Lemma
B.3
and Lemma
C.2
, we have that

(67)

\displaystyle\tr[\cH[a+\ad](\rho)f(N)]

\displaystyle=i\tr[\rho\left(f(N)(a+a^{\dagger})-(a+a^{\dagger})f(N)\right)]

\displaystyle=\tr[\rho\left(-iag_{1}(N)+ig_{1}(N)a^{\dagger}\right)]

\displaystyle\leq 2\,\tr[\rho g_{1}(N+\mathbbm{1})\sqrt{N+\mathbbm{1}}]

\displaystyle\leq 2k\tr[\rho(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2-\frac{1}{2}}]\,,

where we recall that

(68)

g_{1}(x)=\begin{cases}f(x)-f(x-1)&x\geq 0;\\
0&0>x\,.\end{cases}

Thus,

\displaystyle\tr[(\epsilon\mathcal{H}[a+a^{\dagger}]

\displaystyle+\mathcal{L}[a^{2}-\alpha^{2}])(\rho)(f(N))\big]

\displaystyle\leq-2\tr[\rho\,(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2+1}\,\big]+\left(\Delta_{2}+\varepsilon 2k\right)\,\tr[\rho\,(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2}\big].

for
\nu\geq 1
defined as

\nu=\frac{k}{2}+1

ends the proof of the differential upper bound, and (
66
) follows from Proposition
4.1
.
∎

Interestingly,
Equation

67
shows directly that the displacement operator satisfies Assumption
2
so that Theorem
3.1
can be applied.

Corollary 4.8

For any state
\rho\in\mathcal{T}_{f}
,
\alpha\in\mathbb{C}
,
\varepsilon>0
and
k\in\mathbbm{N}

\tr\Bigl[\mathcal{H}[a+a^{\dagger}](N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2}\Bigr]\leq 2k\tr[\rho\,(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/2}\big]

Therefore,
\mathcal{H}[a+a^{\dagger}]
generates a Sobolev and positivity-preserving quantum Markov semigroup.

Nevertheless, the above result is not of the form given in
Equation

47
so the improvement
Equation

48
is not applicable. In the context of bosonic error correction, the projective Zeno effect wr.r.t. the semigroup above and the projection onto the code space (
54
) helps to understand the
Z(\theta)
-gate mathematically
[
50
]
. A quantitative convergence rate can be proven via
[
51
]
.

Proposition 4.9

(CNOT-gate)

For all
\textbf{k}\coloneqq(k_{1},k_{2})\in\mathbbm{N}^{2}
such that

32|\alpha|k_{1}2^{k_{1}/2-1/2}\leq k_{2}\,,

there exists a constant
\mu_{\textbf{k}}
such that for all states
\rho\in\mathcal{T}_{f}

\displaystyle\tr[\left(\mathcal{L}[a^{2}-\alpha^{2}]+\mathcal{L}[b^{2}-\alpha^{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}(1-e^{2i\pi t/T})(a-\alpha)]\right)(\rho)(N_{1}+\mathbbm{1})^{k_{1}/2}(N_{2}+\mathbbm{1})^{k_{2}/2}]

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1+k_{2}}{8}\tr[\rho\Bigl((N_{1}+\mathbbm{1})^{k_{1}/2}(N_{2}+\mathbbm{1})^{k_{2}/2}\Bigr)]+\mu_{\textbf{k}}\,.

Therefore, the CNOT-gate generates a Sobolev and positivity preserving quantum Markov semigroup which satisfies for all states
\rho\in W^{\textbf{k},1}

(69)

\|\mathcal{P}^{\operatorname{CNOT}}_{t,t_{0}}(\rho)\|_{W^{\textbf{k},1}}\leq\max\left\{\|\rho\|_{W^{\textbf{k},1}},\frac{8\mu_{\textbf{k}}}{1+k_{2}}\right\}\,.

For a general
\textbf{k}\in\mathbbm{R}_{+}^{2}
and
x\in W^{\textbf{k},1}
one obtains

\|\mathcal{P}^{\operatorname{CNOT}}_{t,t_{0}}(x)\|_{W^{\textbf{k}_{r},1}}\leq\gamma_{k}\|x\|_{W^{\textbf{k}_{r},1}}\,,

where
\gamma_{\textbf{k}}=\max\{1,\frac{8\mu_{\textbf{k}}}{1+k_{2}}\}
for
\textbf{k}\in\{\textbf{k}_{r}\}_{r\in\mathbbm{N}}
and an interpolated constant in all other cases.

Proof.

We denote
f(x_{1},x_{2})=f_{1}(x_{1})f(x_{2})
with
f_{1}(x_{1})=(x_{1}+1)^{k_{1}/2}1_{x_{1}\geq-1}
,
f_{2}(x_{2})=(x_{2}+1)^{k_{2}/2}1_{x_{2}\geq-1}
, and rewrite the CNOT-generator as

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\coloneqq\mathcal{L}[a^{2}-\alpha^{2}]

\displaystyle+\mathcal{L}[b^{2}-\alpha^{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}(1-e^{2i\pi t/T})(a-\alpha)]=\mathcal{L}[a^{2}-\alpha^{2}]+\mathcal{L}[b^{2}+za+w]

where
z\coloneqq-\frac{\alpha}{2}(1-e^{2i\pi t/T})
and
w\coloneqq-\alpha(z+\alpha)
. As in the previous proofs, we investigate the action of the adjoint on
f(N)\coloneqq f(N_{1},N_{2})

\tr[\mathcal{L}(\rho)f(N)]=\tr[\rho\mathcal{L}^{\dagger}(f(N))]\,.

We first focus on the second Lindbladian
\mathcal{L}[b^{2}+za+w]
: we first consider, for
n\coloneqq(n_{1},n_{2})\in\mathbbm{N}^{2}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}[b^{2}+za+w]^{\dagger}(\outerproduct{n}{n})

\displaystyle=((b^{\dagger})^{2}+\overline{z}a^{\dagger}+\overline{w})\outerproduct{n}{n}(b^{2}+za+w)-\frac{1}{2}\left\{((b^{\dagger})^{2}+\overline{z}a^{\dagger}+\overline{w})(b^{2}+za+w),\outerproduct{n}{n}\right\}

\displaystyle=F_{1}(n)\outerproduct{n}{n}+F_{2}(n)\outerproduct{n_1,n_2+2}{n_1,n_2+2}+F_{3}(n)\outerproduct{n_1+1,n_2}{n_1+1,n_2}

\displaystyle+\big(F_{4}(n)\outerproduct{n_1,n_2+2}{n_1+1,n_2}+h.c.\big)+\big(F_{5}(n)\outerproduct{n_1+1,n_2-2}{n}+h.c.\big)

\displaystyle+\big(F_{6}(n)\outerproduct{n_1,n_2-2}{n}+h.c.\big)+\big(F_{7}(n)\outerproduct{n_1-1,n_2}{n}+h.c.\big)

\displaystyle+\big(F_{8}(n)\outerproduct{n_1,n_2+2}{n}+h.c.\big)+\big(F_{9}(n)\outerproduct{n_1+1,n_2}{n}+h.c.\big)

\displaystyle+\big(F_{10}(n)\outerproduct{n_1-1,n_2+2}{n}+h.c.\big)\,,

where the notation
h.c.
above stands for Hermitian conjugate,
|n\rangle=0
whenever
n\notin\mathbb{N}^{2}
by convention, and where

\displaystyle F_{1}(n)\coloneqq-n_{2}(n_{2}-1)-|z|^{2}n_{1}

\displaystyle F_{2}(n)\coloneqq(n_{2}+1)(n_{2}+2)

\displaystyle F_{3}(n)\coloneqq|z|^{2}(n_{1}+1)

\displaystyle F_{4}(n)\coloneqq z\sqrt{(n_{1}+1)(n_{2}+1)(n_{2}+2)}

\displaystyle F_{5}(n)\coloneqq-\frac{1}{2}\overline{z}\sqrt{(n_{1}+1)n_{2}(n_{2}-1)}

\displaystyle F_{6}(n)\coloneqq-\frac{1}{2}\overline{w}\sqrt{n_{2}(n_{2}-1)}

\displaystyle F_{7}(n)\coloneqq-\frac{1}{2}\overline{w}z\sqrt{n_{1}}

\displaystyle F_{8}(n)\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}w\sqrt{(n_{2}+1)(n_{2}+2)}

\displaystyle F_{9}(n)\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}\overline{z}w\sqrt{n_{1}+1}

\displaystyle F_{10}(n)\coloneqq-\frac{1}{2}z\sqrt{n_{1}(n_{2}+1)(n_{2}+2)}\,.

In the next step, we regroup the
17
terms into terms differing only by a shift:

Case 0:
Diagonal terms, involving
F_{1}
,
F_{2}
and
F_{3}
,

\displaystyle C_{0}(n)\coloneqq F_{1}(n)\outerproduct{n}{n}+F_{2}(n)\outerproduct{n_1,n_2+2}{n_1,n_2+2}+F_{3}(n)\outerproduct{n_1+1,n_2}{n_1+1,n_2}\,.

Case 1:
Terms of the form
\outerproduct{n_1+1,n_2-2}{n_1,n_2}
, involving
\overline{F}_{4}
,
F_{5}
and
\overline{F}_{10}
,

\displaystyle C_{1}(n)\coloneqq\overline{F}_{4}(n)

\displaystyle\outerproduct{n_1+1,n_2}{n_1,n_2+2}

\displaystyle+F_{5}(n)\outerproduct{n_1+1,n_2-2}{n}+\overline{F}_{10}(n)\outerproduct{n}{n_1-1,n_2+2}\,.

Case 1’:
Terms of the form
\outerproduct{n_1,n_2}{n_1+1,n_2-2}
, involving
F4
,
\overline{F}_{5}
and
F_{10}
,

\displaystyle C_{1^{\prime}}(n)\coloneqq{F}_{4}(n)

\displaystyle\outerproduct{n_1,n_2+2}{n_1+1,n_2}

\displaystyle+\overline{F}_{5}(n)\outerproduct{n}{n_1+1,n_2-2}+{F}_{10}(n)\outerproduct{n_1-1,n_2+2}{n}\,.

Case 2:
Terms of the form
\outerproduct{n_1,n_2-2}{n_1,n_2}
, involving
F_{6}
and
\overline{F}_{8}
,

\displaystyle C_{2}(n)\coloneqq{F}_{6}(n)\outerproduct{n_1,n_2-2}{n}+\overline{F}_{8}(n)\outerproduct{n}{n_1,n_2+2}\,.

Case 2’:
Terms of the form
\outerproduct{n_1,n_2}{n_1,n_2-2}
, involving
\overline{F}_{6}
and
{F}_{8}
,

\displaystyle C_{2^{\prime}}(n)\coloneqq\overline{F}_{6}(n)\outerproduct{n}{n_1,n_2-2}+{F}_{8}(n)\outerproduct{n_1,n_2+2}{n}\,.

Case 3:
Terms of the form
\outerproduct{n_1-1,n_2}{n_1,n_2}
, involving
F_{7}
and
\overline{F}_{9}
,

\displaystyle C_{3}(n)\coloneqq{F}_{7}(n)\outerproduct{n_1-1,n_2}{n}+\overline{F}_{9}(n)\outerproduct{n}{n_1+1,n_2}\,.

Case 3’:
Terms of the form
\outerproduct{n_1,n_2}{n_1-1,n_2}
, involving
\overline{F}_{7}
and
{F}_{9}
,

\displaystyle C_{3^{\prime}}(n)\coloneqq\overline{F}_{7}(n)\outerproduct{n}{n_1-1,n_2}+{F}_{9}(n)\outerproduct{n_1+1,n_2}{n}\,.

To summarize, we have decomposed
\mathcal{L}[b^{2}+za+w]^{\dagger}(\outerproduct{n}{n})
into the sum

(70)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}[b^{2}+za+w]^{\dagger}(|n\rangle\langle n|)=C_{0}(n)+C_{1}(n)+C_{1^{\prime}}(n)+C_{2}(n)+C_{2^{\prime}}(n)+C_{3}(n)+C_{3^{\prime}}(n)\,.

Next, we introduce the functions
g_{j,l}:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{R}
,
j\in\{1,2\}
,
l\in\mathbb{N}
, as

g_{j,l}(x)=\begin{cases}f_{j}(x)-f_{j}(x-l)&x\geq l;\\
f_{j}(x)&l>x\geq 0;\\
0&0>x\,.\end{cases}

Multiplying
Equation

70
by
f(n)
and summing over
n\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
, we find that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}[b^{2}+za+w]^{\dagger}(f(N))=\hat{C}_{0}+\hat{C}_{1}+\hat{C}_{1^{\prime}}+\hat{C}_{2}+\hat{C}_{2^{\prime}}+\hat{C}_{3}+\hat{C}_{3^{\prime}}\,,

with

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{0}\coloneqq\sum_{n}\,C_{0}(n)=\sum_{n}-\,\Big(f_{1}(n_{1})g_{2,2}(n_{2})n_{2}(n_{2}-1)+f_{2}(n_{2})g_{1,1}(n_{1})|z|^{2}n_{1}\Big)\outerproduct{n}{n}

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{1}\coloneqq\sum_{n}\,C_{1}(n)

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{n}\,-\frac{\overline{z}}{2}\sqrt{(n_{1}+1)(n_{2}-1)n_{2}}\Bigl(f_{1}(n_{1})g_{2,2}(n_{2})+g_{1,1}(n_{1}+1)f_{2}(n_{2}-2)\Bigr)\outerproduct{n_1+1,n_2-2}{n}

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{1^{\prime}}\coloneqq\hat{C}_{1}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{n}\,-\frac{{z}}{2}\sqrt{(n_{1}+1)(n_{2}-1)n_{2}}\Bigl(f_{1}(n_{1})g_{2,2}(n_{2})+g_{1,1}(n_{1}+1)f_{2}(n_{2}-2)\Bigr)\outerproduct{n}{n_1+1,n_2-2}

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{2}\coloneqq\sum_{n}\,C_{2}(n)=\sum_{n}-\frac{\overline{w}}{2}\sqrt{(n_{2}-1)n_{2}}f_{1}(n_{1})g_{2,2}(n_{2})\outerproduct{n_1,n_2-2}{n}

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{2^{\prime}}\coloneqq\hat{C}_{2}^{\dagger}=\sum_{n}-\frac{{w}}{2}\sqrt{(n_{2}-1)n_{2}}f_{1}(n_{1})g_{2,2}(n_{2})\outerproduct{n}{n_1,n_2-2}

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{3}\coloneqq\sum_{n}\,C_{3}(n)=\sum_{n}-\frac{\overline{w}z}{2}\sqrt{n_{1}}g_{1,1}(n_{1})f_{2}(n_{2})\outerproduct{n_1-1,n_2}{n}

\displaystyle\hat{C}_{3^{\prime}}\coloneqq\hat{C}_{3}^{\dagger}=\sum_{n}-\frac{{w}\overline{z}}{2}\sqrt{n_{1}}g_{1,1}(n_{1})f_{2}(n_{2})\outerproduct{n}{n_1-1,n_2}\,.

We will use an upper bound on
\hat{C}_{0}(n)
in what follows:

\displaystyle C_{0}(n)=-\Big(f_{1}(n_{1})g_{2,2}(n_{2})(n_{2}-1)n_{2}+|z|^{2}g_{1,1}(n_{1})f_{2}(n_{2})n_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq-\Big(f_{1}(n_{1})g_{2,2}(n_{2})1_{n_{2}\geq 2}((n_{2}+1)^{2}-3(n_{2}+1))+|z|^{2}g_{1,1}(n_{1})f_{2}(n_{2})n_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{(1)}{\leq}-\Bigl\{k_{2}f_{1}(n_{1})(n_{2}+1)^{k_{2}/2}1_{n_{2}\geq 2}\Big((n_{2}+1)-1_{k_{2}\geq 3}\frac{k_{2}}{2}-6\Big)

\displaystyle+|z|^{2}1_{n_{1}\geq 1}(n_{1}+1)^{k_{1}/2-1}f_{2}(n_{2})n_{1}\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\equiv C_{0^{\prime}}(n)\,,

where
(1)
follows from Lemma
C.2
. We denote this upper bound by
\hat{C}_{0^{\prime}}=\sum_{n}C_{0^{\prime}}(n)\outerproduct{n}{n}
. Recall that, by Lemma
4.3
and Remark
7
,

(71)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}[a^{2}-\alpha^{2}]^{\dagger}(f(N))

\displaystyle\leq-\,(N_{1}+\mathbbm{1})^{k_{1}/2+1}(N_{2}+\mathbbm{1})^{k_{2}/2}+\mu_{k_{1}}^{(2)}\,(N_{2}+\mathbbm{1})^{k_{2}/2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n}\Big(-(n_{1}+1)f(n)+\mu_{k_{1}}^{(2)}\,f_{2}(n_{2})\Big)\outerproduct{n}{n}=:\hat{C}_{4}\,,

where
\mu_{k_{1}}^{(2)}=\Delta_{2}^{\nu}\left(\frac{(\nu-1)^{\nu-1}}{\nu^{\nu}}\right)
with
\nu=\frac{k_{1}}{2}+1
and
\Delta_{2}=6+2|\alpha|^{2}k_{1}2^{k_{1}/2-1}\sqrt{2}
. Therefore, the diagonal contribution of
\mathcal{L}^{\dagger}(f(N))
can be controlled by

\displaystyle({C}_{0^{\prime}}+{C}_{4})(n)

\displaystyle\coloneqq-\biggl(k_{2}f_{1}(n_{1})(n_{2}+1)^{k_{2}/2+1}1_{n_{2}\geq 2}+|z|^{2}1_{n_{1}\geq 1}f(n)+(n_{1}+1)^{k_{1}/2+1}f_{2}(n_{2})\biggr)

\displaystyle+k_{2}f_{1}(n_{1})(n_{2}+1)^{k_{2}/2}1_{n_{2}\geq 2}\biggl(1_{k_{2}\geq 3}\frac{k_{2}}{2}+6\biggr)

\displaystyle+|z|^{2}1_{n_{1}\geq 1}(n_{1}+1)^{k_{2}/2-1}f_{2}(n_{2})

\displaystyle+\mu_{k_{1}}^{(2)}(n_{2}+1)^{k_{2}/2}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{2}\biggl(k_{2}f_{1}(n_{1})(n_{2}+1)^{k_{2}/2+1}+(n_{1}+1)^{k_{1}/2+1}f_{2}(n_{2})\biggr)+\Delta_{0}

\displaystyle\eqqcolon-x(n)

where the constant
\Delta_{0}
is achieved by splitting off half of the negative leading order terms to control the lower order positive contributions (see for example the proof of Lemma
4.3
).
Next, we consider operators of the form

(72)

-x_{1}\outerproduct{e_1}{e_1}-x_{2}\outerproduct{e_2}{e_2}+y\outerproduct{e_2}{e_1}+\overline{y}\outerproduct{e_1}{e_2}\,,

where
\{e_{1},e_{2}\}
forms an orthonormal basis of a two-dimensional Hilbert space and
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbbm{R},y\in\mathbbm{C}
. The operator in
Equation

72
has the eigenvalues

(73)

\lambda_{+}=\frac{-x_{1}-x_{2}+\sqrt{(x_{1}-x_{2})^{2}+4|y|^{2}}}{2},\quad\lambda_{-}=\frac{-x_{1}-x_{2}-\sqrt{(x_{1}-x_{2})^{2}+4|y|^{2}}}{2}

Moreover,

\frac{-x_{1}-x_{2}+\sqrt{(x_{1}-x_{2})^{2}+4|y|^{2}}}{2}\leq-\min\{x_{1},x_{2}\}+|y|\,.

Using this bound, we control each of the off-diagonal operators
\hat{C}_{i}+\hat{C}_{i^{\prime}}
,
i\in\{1,2,3\}
, in terms of
\frac{1}{4}X
, where
X=\sum_{n}x(n)\outerproduct{n}{n}
.

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}X+\hat{C}_{1}+\hat{C}_{1^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{n}-\frac{1}{4}x(n)\outerproduct{n}{n}+y_{1}\outerproduct{n}{n_1+1,n_2-2}+\overline{y}_{1}\outerproduct{n_1+1,n_2-2}{n}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n|n_{2}\geq 2}-\frac{1}{8}x(n)\outerproduct{n}{n}-\frac{1}{8}x(n_{1}+1,n_{2}-2)\outerproduct{n_1+1,n_2-2}{n_1+1,n_2-2}

\displaystyle+y_{1}\outerproduct{n}{n_1+1,n_2-2}+\overline{y}_{1}\outerproduct{n_1+1,n_2-2}{n}

with

y_{1}=y_{1}(n_{1},n_{2})=-\frac{{z}}{2}\sqrt{(n_{1}+1)(n_{2}-1)n_{2}}\Bigl(f_{1}(n_{1})g_{2,2}(n_{2})+g_{1,1}(n_{1}+1)f_{2}(n_{2}-2)\Bigr)

so that

(74)

-\frac{1}{4}X+\hat{C}_{1}+\hat{C}_{1^{\prime}}\leq\sum_{n|n_{2}\geq 2}\left(-\min\{x_{1},x_{2}\}+|y_{1}|\right)(\outerproduct{n}{n}+\outerproduct{n_1 + 1, n_2 - 2}{n_1 + 1, n_2 - 2})

where
x_{1}=\frac{1}{8}x(n_{1},n_{2})
and
x_{2}=\frac{1}{8}x(n_{1}+1,n_{2}-2)
. Moreover, for
n_{2}\geq 2

\displaystyle|y_{1}|

\displaystyle\overset{(1)}{\leq}\frac{|z|}{2}\sqrt{(n_{1}+1)(n_{2}-1)n_{2}}\Bigl(f_{1}(n_{1})2k_{2}(n_{2}+1)^{k_{2}/2-1}+k_{1}(n_{1}+2)^{k_{1}/2-1}f_{2}(n_{2}-2)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq|z|k_{2}(n_{1}+1)^{k_{1}/2+1/2}(n_{2}+1)^{k_{2}/2}

\displaystyle+\frac{|z|k_{1}}{2}\sqrt{(n_{2}-1)^{2}+n_{2}-1}\,(n_{1}+2)^{k_{1}/2-1/2}f_{2}(n_{2}-2)

\displaystyle\leq|z|k_{2}(n_{1}+1)^{k_{1}/2+1/2}(n_{2}+1)^{k_{2}/2}+|z|k_{1}\,(n_{1}+2)^{k_{1}/2-1/2}(n_{2}-1)^{k_{2}/2+1}\,,

where
(1)
follows from Lemma
C.2
. At this stage, we consider two cases:

Case (i):
x_{2}\geq x_{1}
. In that case,
-\min\{x_{1},x_{2}\}+|y_{1}|=-x_{1}+|y_{1}|
, and therefore

\displaystyle-\min\{x_{1},x_{2}\}+|y_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{16}\biggl(k_{2}f_{1}(n_{1})(n_{2}+1)^{k_{2}/2+1}+(n_{1}+1)^{k_{1}/2+1}f_{2}(n_{2})\biggr)+\frac{1}{8}\Delta_{0}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{|z|k_{2}(n_{1}+1)^{k_{1}/2+1/2}(n_{2}+1)^{k_{2}/2}}_{=:A_{1}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{|z|k_{1}\,(n_{1}+2)^{k_{1}/2-1/2}(n_{2}-1)^{k_{2}/2+1}}_{=:A_{2}}\,.

Note that the first positive non-constant term
A_{1}
can be controlled with half the negative contribution in the first term by a constant using the same type of polynomial optimization as in the proof of Lemma
4.3
. For the last term, i.e.
A_{2}
, we use the assumption

(75)

|z|k_{1}2^{k_{1}/2-1/2}\leq|\alpha|k_{1}2^{k_{1}/2-1/2}\leq\frac{1}{32}k_{2},

which allows us to control
A_{2}
with the other half of the first term, as we already did with
A_{1}
. Recall the definition
z=-\frac{\alpha}{2}(1-e^{2i\pi t/T})
. Summarising the above considerations we can conclude the existence of a constant
\tilde{\Delta}^{\prime}_{1}
such that

\displaystyle-\min\{x_{1},x_{2}\}+|y_{1}|\leq\tilde{\Delta}^{\prime}_{1}\,.

Case (ii):
x_{2}\leq x_{1}
. In that case
-\min\{x_{1},x_{2}\}+|y_{1}|=-x_{2}+|y_{1}|
, and therefore

\displaystyle-\min\{x_{1},x_{2}\}+|y_{1}|

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{16}\biggl(k_{2}f_{1}(n_{1}+1)(n_{2}-1)^{k_{2}/2+1}+(n_{1}+2)^{k_{1}/2+1}f_{2}(n_{2}-2)\biggr)+\frac{1}{8}\Delta_{0}

\displaystyle+|z|k_{2}(n_{1}+1)^{k_{1}/2+1/2}(n_{2}+1)^{k_{2}/2}

\displaystyle+|z|k_{1}\,(n_{1}+2)^{k_{1}/2-1/2}(n_{2}-1)^{k_{2}/2+1}\,.

To upper bound the above, we use again the assumption (
75
), which implies the existence of a constant
\tilde{\Delta}^{\prime}_{1}
such that

\displaystyle-\min\{x_{1},x_{2}\}+|y_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\Delta}^{\prime}_{1}\,.

Combining cases (i) and (ii) above, denoting
\Delta_{1}\coloneqq\max\{\tilde{\Delta}_{1},\tilde{\Delta}_{1}^{\prime}\}
and plugging the bounds into (
74
), we arrive at

(76)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}X+\hat{C}_{1}+\hat{C}_{1^{\prime}}\leq\Delta_{1}\sum\limits_{n|n_{2}\geq 2}(\outerproduct{n}{n}+\outerproduct{n_1 + 1, n_2 - 2}{n_1 + 1, n_2 - 2})

Next, we control
-\frac{1}{4}X+\hat{C}_{2}+\hat{C}_{2^{\prime}}
. Here, we have

y_{2}=y_{2}(n_{1},n_{2})=-\frac{\overline{w}}{2}\sqrt{(n_{2}-1)n_{2}}f_{1}(n_{1})g_{2,2}(n_{2})\,,

x_{1}=\frac{1}{8}x(n)
, and
x_{2}=\frac{1}{8}x(n_{1},n_{2}-2)
. By Lemma
C.2
, we have that

\displaystyle|y_{2}|

\displaystyle\leq{|{w}|}\sqrt{(n_{2}-1)n_{2}}f_{1}(n_{1})k_{2}(n_{2}+1)^{k_{2}/2-1}\,.

Therefore, the negative contribution from
\min\{x_{1},x_{2}\}
has leading order in both variables
n_{1}
and
n_{2}
, which implies the existence of a constant
\Delta_{2}
such that

-\min\{x_{1},x_{2}\}+|y_{2}|\leq\Delta_{2}\,.

Hence,

(77)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}X+\hat{C}_{2}+\hat{C}_{2^{\prime}}\leq\Delta_{2}\sum\limits_{n|n_{2}\geq 2}(\outerproduct{n}{n}+\outerproduct{n_1, n_2 - 2}{n_1, n_2 - 2})

Finally, we consider
-\frac{1}{4}X+\hat{C}_{3}+\hat{C}_{3^{\prime}}
. In this case,

y_{3}=y_{3}(n_{1},n_{2})=-\frac{\overline{w}z}{2}\sqrt{n_{1}}g_{1,1}(n_{1})f_{2}(n_{2})\,,

x_{1}=\frac{1}{8}x(n)
, and
x_{2}=\frac{1}{8}x(n_{1}-1,n_{2})
. Similarly to the above, we can argue the existence of a constant
\Delta_{3}
such that

-\max\{x_{1},x_{2}\}+|y_{3}|\leq\Delta_{3}\,.

Hence,

(78)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}X+\hat{C}_{3}+\hat{C}_{3^{\prime}}\leq\Delta_{3}\sum\limits_{n|n_{1}\geq 1}(\outerproduct{n}{n}+\outerproduct{n_1 - 1, n_2}{n_1 - 1, n_2}

Combining (
76
), (
77
) and (
78
), we have shown that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\dagger}(f(N))

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{X}{4}+\Delta_{1}\sum_{n|n_{2}\geq 2}(\outerproduct{n}{n}+\outerproduct{n_1 + 1, n_2 - 2}{n_1 + 1, n_2 - 2})

\displaystyle+\Delta_{2}\sum_{n|n_{2}\geq 2}(\outerproduct{n}{n}+\outerproduct{n_1, n_2 - 2}{n_1, n_2 - 2})

\displaystyle+\Delta_{3}\sum\limits_{n|n_{1}\geq 1}(\outerproduct{n}{n}+\outerproduct{n_1 - 1, n_2}{n_1 - 1, n_2})

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{X}{4}+2(\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}+\Delta_{3})\mathbbm{1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{8}\Big(k_{2}f_{1}(N_{1})(N_{2}+1)^{k_{2}/2+1}+(N_{1}+1)^{k_{1}/2+1}f_{2}(N_{2})\Big)+\mu_{\textbf{k}}\,\mathbbm{1}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1+k_{2}}{8}f(N)+\mu_{\textbf{k}}\mathbbm{1}\,,

with

\mu_{\textbf{k}}\coloneqq\frac{\Delta_{0}}{4}+2(\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}+\Delta_{3})\,.

The claim (
69
) finally follows from Proposition
4.1
.
∎

5
Perturbation bounds

In this section, we establish a perturbative analysis at any time scale for the semigroups considered in Section
4
. In finite dimensions,
[
65
, Theorem 6]
gives a quantitative bound which controls the perturbation of a quantum dynamical semigroup under the condition that the latter converges exponentially fast to a unique invariant state
\tau
: for two generators
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{L}+\mathcal{K}
, if
\mathcal{L}
satisfies
\|e^{t\mathcal{L}}-\tr(.)\,\tau\|_{1\rightarrow 1}\leq ce^{-\omega t}
for all
t\geq 0
and some
c,\omega>0
, then

\forall\rho,\sigma\text{ states},\quad\norm{e^{t\cL}(\rho) - e^{t(\cL+\cK)}(\sigma)}_{1}\leq\begin{cases}\norm{\rho- \sigma}_{1}+t\norm{\cK}_{1\rightarrow 1}\,,&t<\hat{t}\\
ce^{-\omega t}\norm{\rho- \sigma}_{1}+\frac{\log(c)+1-ce^{-\omega t}}{\omega}\norm{ \cK}_{1\rightarrow 1}\,,&t\geq\hat{t}\end{cases}

where
\hat{t}\coloneqq\frac{\log(c)}{\omega}
. The result can be easily extended to the case of bounded generators in infinite dimensions, although proving the exponential decay for the semigroup generated by
\mathcal{L}
is not easy. The situation becomes even trickier in the case of unbounded generators since the use of a Duhamel integral as in the proof in finite dimensions requires a proper justification. It is precisely these issues that we are interested in and want to address here.

5.1
Gaussian perturbations of the quantum Ornstein Uhlenbeck semigroup

The quantum Ornstein Uhlenbeck semigroup is well-known to correspond to a so-called beam-splitter channel of exponentially decreasing transmissivity
e^{-(\lambda^{2}-\mu^{2})t}
with unique Gaussian invariant state (see
[
21
]
):

\sigma\coloneqq\frac{\lambda^{2}-\mu^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\mu^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\right)^{k}\,\outerproduct{k}{k}\,.

While quantitative statements about the convergence of this semigroup towards
\sigma
are known
[
18
,
13
,
14
,
21
]
,
they do not necessarily imply convergence in trace distance in contrast to their finite-dimensional analogues. In contrast, the semigroup is known to contract a certain kind of quantum Wasserstein distance, which we introduce now. First, given a bounded, self-adjoint operator
X\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H})
, we call
X
a Lipschitz observable if
aX
,
a^{\dagger}X
are bounded, and if
Xa
and
Xa^{\dagger}
are closable operators with bounded closures
\overline{Xa}
and
\overline{Xa^{\dagger}}
. In this case, we denote by
\partial_{a}(X)\coloneqq aX-\overline{Xa}
and
\partial_{a^{\dagger}}(X)=a^{\dagger}X-\overline{Xa^{\dagger}}
. The Lipschitz constant of
X
is then defined as

\displaystyle\|X\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}\coloneqq\max\big\{\|\partial_{a}(X)\|_{\infty},\,\|\partial_{a^{\dagger}}(X)\|_{\infty}\big\}\,.

We denote the set of Lipschitz observables by
\operatorname{Lip}
. Next, any
T\in\mathcal{T}_{1,\operatorname{sa}}
, we denote

\displaystyle\|T\|_{W_{1}}\coloneqq\sup\,\left\{\tr[X\,T\big]:\,X\in\operatorname{Lip},\,\|X\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}\leq 1\right\}\,.

In
[
28
, Proposition 6.4]
, the authors showed that, for any
T\in\mathcal{T}_{1,\operatorname{sa}}
and
t>0
,

(79)

\displaystyle\|e^{t\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{qOU}}}(T)\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{\frac{e^{-(\lambda^{2}-\mu^{2})t}}{1-e^{-(\lambda^{2}-\mu^{2})t}}}\,\Big(\|a\sigma-\sigma a\|_{1}+\|a^{\dagger}\sigma-\sigma a^{\dagger}\|_{1}\Big)\,\|T\|_{W_{1}}\,.

Moreover, using the canonical commutation relations, one can also prove the following identities (see e.g.
[
14
]
, or
[
28
, Proposition 6.2]
): for any two states
\rho_{1},\rho_{2}\in\mathcal{T}_{1,\operatorname{sa}}
,

(80)

\|e^{t\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{qOU}}}(\rho_{1}-\rho_{2})\|_{W_{1}}\leq e^{-\frac{(\lambda^{2}-\mu^{2})t}{2}}\,\|\rho_{1}-\rho_{2}\|_{W_{1}}\,.

In the next proposition, we use these conditions to find a perturbation bound for any Gaussian perturbation of the quantum Ornstein Uhlenbeck semigroup.

Proposition 5.1

Let
(\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{qOU}},\mathcal{T}_{f})
be the generator of the quantum Ornstein Uhlenbeck semigroup with
\lambda>\mu\geq 0
and
(\varepsilon\mathcal{L}_{G},\mathcal{T}_{f})\coloneqq(\varepsilon\mathcal{L}[{\gamma a+\eta a^{\dagger}}],\mathcal{T}_{f})
a Gaussian perturbation with
\gamma,\eta\in\mathbb{R}
,
\varepsilon>0
. Then, assuming
\lambda^{2}-\mu^{2}+|\gamma|^{2}-|\eta|^{2}>0
,
\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{qOU}}+\varepsilon\mathcal{L}_{G}
generates a positivity and Sobolev preserving semigroup on
W^{k,1}
for
k\geq 1
, and there exist uniformly bounded functions
C(\varepsilon),D(\varepsilon)
depending on
\lambda,\mu,|\eta|,|\gamma|
such that, for all
t\geq 0
and states
\rho\in W^{2,1}

(81)

\Big\|\left(e^{t\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{qOU}}}-e^{t(\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{qOU}}+\varepsilon\mathcal{L}_{G})}\right)(\rho)\Big\|_{1}\leq\varepsilon\,C(\varepsilon)\,\max\Big\{\norm{\rho}_{W^{2,1}},D(\varepsilon)\Big\}\,.

Proof.

The generation of a Sobolev preserving semigroup was already stated in Lemma
4.2
for
\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{qOU}}
and its proof can easily be extended to
\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{qOU}}+\varepsilon\mathcal{L}_{G}
. For instance, given a state
\rho\in\mathcal{T}_{f}
, one can show that

\tr[\mathcal{L}_{G}(\rho)(N+\mathbbm{1})]\leq-(|\gamma|^{2}-|\eta|^{2})\,\tr[\rho N]+|\eta|^{2}\,,

We have also seen in the proof of Lemma
4.2
that
\tr[\rho\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{qOU}}]\leq-(\lambda^{2}-\mu^{2})\tr[\rho N]+\mu^{2}
, so that

\displaystyle\tr[(\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{qOU}}+\varepsilon\mathcal{L}_{G})(\rho)(N+\mathbbm{1})]\leq-(\lambda^{2}-\mu^{2}+\varepsilon|\gamma|^{2}-\varepsilon|\eta|^{2})\tr[\rho(N+\mathbbm{1})]+\lambda^{2}+\varepsilon|\gamma|^{2}\,.

Therefore, by Proposition
4.1
we have that, as long as
\lambda^{2}-\mu^{2}+\varepsilon|\gamma|^{2}-\varepsilon|\eta|^{2}>0
, for all states
\rho\in W^{2,1}
,
\rho\geq 0
and all
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\|e^{t(\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{qOU}}+\varepsilon\mathcal{L}_{G})}(\rho)\|_{W^{2,1}}\leq\max\left\{\norm{\rho}_{W^{2,1}},\frac{\lambda^{2}+\varepsilon|\gamma|^{2}}{\lambda^{2}-\mu^{2}+\varepsilon|\gamma|^{2}-\varepsilon|\eta|^{2}}\right\}\,.

Next, for
\rho\in\mathcal{T}_{f}
and
0<u<t
, and denoting
\mathcal{L}\equiv\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{qOU}}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\equiv\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{qOU}}+\varepsilon\mathcal{L}_{G}
,

\displaystyle\Big\|\Big(e^{t\mathcal{L}}-e^{t\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}}\Big)(\rho)\Big\|_{1}\leq\Big\|e^{u\mathcal{L}}\left(e^{(t-u)\mathcal{L}}-e^{(t-u)\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)(\rho)\Big\|_{1}+\Big\|\Big(e^{u\mathcal{L}}-e^{u\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}}\Big)e^{(t-u)\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}}(\rho)\Big\|_{1}\equiv A+B\,.

We use
Equation

79
, so that

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle\leq c_{u}\Big\|\Big(e^{(t-u)\mathcal{L}}-e^{(t-u)\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}}\Big)(\rho)\Big\|_{W_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{u}\,\varepsilon\,\int_{0}^{t-u}\,\Big\|e^{s\mathcal{L}}\mathcal{L}_{G}\,e^{(t-u-s)\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}}(\rho)\Big\|_{W_{1}}\,ds

\displaystyle=c_{u}\,\varepsilon\,\int_{0}^{t-u}\,e^{-\frac{(\lambda^{2}-\mu^{2})s}{2}}\Big\|\mathcal{L}_{G}\,e^{(t-u-s)\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}}(\rho)\Big\|_{W_{1}}\,ds\,,

where
c_{u}\coloneqq\sqrt{\frac{e^{-(\lambda^{2}-\mu^{2})u}}{1-e^{-(\lambda^{2}-\mu^{2})u}}}\,\,\Big(\|\partial_{a}(\sigma)\|_{1}+\|\partial_{a^{\dagger}}(\sigma)\|_{1}\Big)
. Moreover, denoting
\widetilde{\rho}_{v}\coloneqq e^{v\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}}(\rho)
and
b=\gamma a+\eta a^{\dagger}
, since
\widetilde{\rho}_{v}\in W^{2,1}
for all
v\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathcal{L}_{G}\,\widetilde{\rho}_{v}\Big\|_{W_{1}}\,

\displaystyle=\sup_{\|X\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}\leq 1}\,\tr[X\mathcal{L}_{G}\widetilde{\rho}_{v}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\,\sup_{\|X\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}\leq 1}\,\tr[\partial_{b^{\dagger}}(X)b\widetilde{\rho}_{v}-\partial_{b}(X)\,\widetilde{\rho}_{v}b^{\dagger}]

\displaystyle\leq(|\eta|+|\gamma|)\,\Big(\|b\widetilde{\rho}_{v}\|_{1}+\|\widetilde{\rho}_{v}b^{\dagger}\|_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq(|\eta|+|\gamma|)\,\Big(\|b\,(N+\mathbbm{1})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|+\|(N+\mathbbm{1})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,b^{\dagger}\|\Big)\,\|\widetilde{\rho}_{v}\|_{W^{2,1}}

\displaystyle\leq(|\eta|+|\gamma|)\Big(\|b\,(N+\mathbbm{1})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|+\|(N+\mathbbm{1})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,b^{\dagger}\|\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\max\left\{\norm{\rho}_{W^{2,1}},\frac{\lambda^{2}+\varepsilon|\gamma|^{2}}{\lambda^{2}-\mu^{2}+\varepsilon|\gamma|^{2}-\varepsilon|\eta|^{2}}\right\}\,.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\,\int_{0}^{u}\,\|e^{s\mathcal{L}}\mathcal{L}_{G}e^{(t-s)\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}}(\rho)\|_{1}\,ds

\displaystyle\leq\,u\varepsilon\,\max_{s\in[0,u]}\,\|\mathcal{L}_{G}e^{(t-s)\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}}(\rho)\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq u\varepsilon\|\mathcal{L}_{G}\circ\mathcal{W}^{-2}\|_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\to\mathcal{T}_{1}}\,\max_{s\in[0,u]}\,\|e^{(t-s)\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}}(\rho)\|_{W^{2,1}}

\displaystyle\leq u\varepsilon\|\mathcal{L}_{G}\circ\mathcal{W}^{-2}\|_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\to\mathcal{T}_{1}}\,\max\left\{\|\rho\|_{W^{2,1}},\frac{\lambda^{2}+\varepsilon|\gamma|^{2}}{\lambda^{2}-\mu^{2}+\varepsilon|\gamma|^{2}-\varepsilon|\eta|^{2}}\right\}\,.

The result follows from a simple bound on
\|\mathcal{L}_{G}\circ\mathcal{W}^{-2}\|_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\to\mathcal{T}_{1}}
with the help of Hölder’s inequality and a standard density argument, and by choosing
u
appropriately.
∎

5.2
Photon-dissipation and CAT qubits

As mentioned before, one crucial property of the underlying evolution in continuous error correction is that it is exponentially converging to the code-space. In the spirit of
[
65
]
, we prove a large time perturbation result for the
l
-photon dissipation perturbed by a Hamiltonian evolution. This can be generalized to dissipative perturbations. First, we recall the exponential convergence of the
l
-photon dissipation defined by
L=a^{l}-\alpha^{l}
(
[
5
, Theorem 2]
):

(82)

\tr[Le^{t\mathcal{L}_{l}}(\rho)L^{\dagger}]\leq e^{-l!t}\tr[L\rho L^{\dagger}]\,.

Additionally, it is shown that there is a unique limit
\overline{\rho}
of
e^{t\mathcal{L}_{l}}(\rho)
for
t\rightarrow\infty
. We show large-time perturbation bounds by combining this bound with our established generation theory for Sobolev and positivity-preserving Markov semigroups. We start with the
l
-photon dissipation perturbed by the Hamiltonian introduced in Lemma
4.3
, i.e.
H=p_{H}(a,a^{\dagger})
with
d_{H}\leq 2(l-1)
.

Theorem 5.2

Let
\mathcal{L}_{l}
be the generator of the
l
-photon dissipation and
p_{H}\in\mathbbm{C}[X,Y]
with
\deg(p_{H})=d_{H}\leq 2(l-1)
such that
H=p_{H}(a,a^{\dagger})
is a symmetric operator. Then, there exist explicit constants
c,\gamma>0
such that for
\varepsilon\geq 0
and all states
\rho\in W^{2(l+d_{H}+2),1}

\displaystyle\Big|\tr[L\left(e^{t\mathcal{L}_{l}}(\rho)-e^{t(\mathcal{L}_{l}+\varepsilon\mathcal{H}[H])}(\rho)\right)L^{\dagger}]\Big|\leq\varepsilon c\left(1-e^{-l!t}\right)\max\{\gamma,\|\rho\|_{W^{2(l+d_{H}+2),1}}\}\,.

Proof.

The proof consists in applying Lemma
4.6
in combination with
Equation

82
. Let
\rho\in\mathcal{T}_{f}
and
\mathcal{W}^{k}=(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/4}\cdot(N+\mathbbm{1})^{k/4}
, then

\displaystyle\tr[L\biggl(e^{t\mathcal{L}_{l}}(\rho)-e^{t(\mathcal{L}_{l}+\varepsilon\mathcal{H}[H])}(\rho)\biggr)L^{\dagger}]

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\tr[Le^{s\mathcal{L}_{l}}\mathcal{W}^{-2(l+2)}\mathcal{W}^{2(l+2)}\mathcal{H}{[H]}e^{(t-s)(\mathcal{L}_{l}+\varepsilon\mathcal{H}[H])}(\rho)L^{\dagger}]ds

\displaystyle\overset{(1)}{\leq}\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\tr[Le^{s\mathcal{L}_{l}}\mathcal{W}^{-2(l+2)}(\mathbbm{1})L^{\dagger}]ds\,2\Lambda d_{H}^{l+2}\sqrt{d_{H}!}\max\left\{\gamma_{\varepsilon},\,\|\rho\|_{W^{2(l+d_{H}+2),1}}\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{(2)}{\leq}\varepsilon\frac{\pi^{2}}{3}\Lambda d_{H}^{2}\sqrt{d_{H}!}(1+|\alpha|^{l}\,(l+1)\,\sqrt{l!}+|\alpha|^{2l})\frac{1}{l!}(1-e^{-l!t})\max\left\{\gamma_{\varepsilon},\,\|\rho\|_{W^{2(l+d_{H}+2),1}}\right\}

\displaystyle\eqqcolon\varepsilon c(1-e^{-l!t})\max\left\{\gamma_{\varepsilon},\,\|\rho\|_{W^{2(l+d_{H}+2),1}}\right\}

where
\Lambda
denotes the larges coefficient of
\mathcal{H}[H]
in absolute value. Note that the Bochner integral in the calculation is well-defined by the boundedness of the integrand w.r.t.
s
and the same argumentation as Theorem
2.11
. Besides the boundedness above, the Sobolev preserving property of the involved semigroups implies by construction that the integral is Sobolev preserving. Therefore, the integral commutes with the map
x\mapsto\tr[LxL^{\dagger}]
for
x\in W^{2(l+d_{H}+2),1}
. In
(1)
we used that
Le^{s\mathcal{L}_{l}}(\cdot)L^{\dagger}
preserves positivity and

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}^{2(l+1)}\mathcal{H}[H]e^{t-s(\mathcal{L}_{l}+\varepsilon\mathcal{H}[H]}(\rho)

\displaystyle\leq\norm{\cW^{2(l + 2)} \cH[H] e^{(t - s)(\cL_l + \varepsilon\cH[H]}(\rho)}_{\infty}\mathbbm{1}

\displaystyle\leq\norm{\cW^{2(l + 2)} \cH[H] e^{(t - s)(\cL_l + \varepsilon\cH[H]}(\rho)}_{1}\mathbbm{1}

\displaystyle\leq 2\Lambda d_{H}^{l+2}\sqrt{d_{H}!}\norm{e^{(t - s)(\cL_l + \varepsilon\cH[H]}(\rho)}_{W^{2(l+d_{H}+2)}}\mathbbm{1}

\displaystyle\leq 2\Lambda d_{H}^{l+2}\sqrt{d_{H}!}\max\{\gamma_{\varepsilon},\norm{\rho}_{W^{2(l+d_{H}+2)}}\}\mathbbm{1}\,.

In the above estimation, we used
(N+\mathbbm{1})^{-d_{H}}
to control
\mathcal{H}[H]
. We further used the Sobolev preserving property of the semigroup from Lemma
4.6
. Finally, applying the decay (q.v.
Equation

82
) to
\mathcal{W}^{-2(l+1)}(\mathbbm{1})=(N+\mathbbm{1})^{-(l+2)}
we estimated in
(2)
:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\tr[Le^{s\mathcal{L}_{l}}\mathcal{W}^{-2(l+2)}(\mathbbm{1})L^{\dagger}]ds

\displaystyle\leq\int\limits_{0}^{t}e^{-l!t}\tr[L(N+\mathbbm{1})^{-(l+2)}L^{\dagger}]

\displaystyle\leq(1-e^{-l!t})(1+|\alpha|^{l}\,(l+1)\,\sqrt{l!}+|\alpha|^{2l})\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}

with the bound

\|L(N+1)^{-l-2}L^{\dagger}\|_{1}\leq(1+|\alpha|^{l}\,(l+1)\,\sqrt{l!}+|\alpha|^{2l})\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}

that follows from

(83)

\displaystyle L^{\dagger}L

\displaystyle=(a^{\dagger})^{l}a^{l}-\overline{\alpha}^{l}a^{l}-\alpha^{l}(a^{\dagger})^{l}+|\alpha|^{2l}

\displaystyle\leq(N-(l-1)\mathbbm{1})\cdots(N-\mathbbm{1})N+|\alpha|^{l}\,(l+1)\,\sqrt{(N+l\mathbbm{1})\cdots(N+\mathbbm{1})}+|\alpha|^{2l}

\displaystyle\leq(N+\mathbbm{1})^{l}+|\alpha|^{l}\,(l+1)\,\sqrt{l!}\,(N+\mathbbm{1})^{l/2}+|\alpha|^{2l}\,.

and the
\norm{(N + \1)^{-2}}_{1}=\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}
. This concludes that claim.
∎

Special cases of the above result include the
X
or
Z(\theta)
gate.

Corollary 5.3

(
Z(\theta)
-gate)

Let
\mathcal{L}_{2}
be the
2
-photon dissipation. Then, for all
\varepsilon\in[0,1]
and all states
\rho\in W^{10,1}

\displaystyle\Big|\tr[L\left(e^{t\mathcal{L}_{2}}(\rho)-e^{t(\mathcal{L}_{2}+\varepsilon\mathcal{H}[a+a^{\dagger}])}(\rho)\right)L^{\dagger}]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon 2(1+6|\alpha|^{2}+|\alpha|^{4})(1-e^{-2t})\max\left\{\gamma_{\varepsilon},\|\rho\|_{W^{10,1}}\right\}

where
\gamma_{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{25}\left(6+\sqrt{2}\,2^{6}\,5\,|\alpha|^{2}+\varepsilon 4^{5}\sqrt{24}\right)^{6}
.

5.3
Application: entropic and capacity continuity bounds

Here, we provide one basic application to the perturbation bounds found in this section. We recall the definition of the energy-constrained diamond norm:

Definition 5.4

(see
[
61
,
68
]
)

Given
E\geq 0
and any two completely positive, trace-preserving maps
\mathcal{N},\mathcal{M}:\mathcal{T}_{1}(\mathcal{H}_{m})\to\mathcal{T}_{1}(\mathcal{H}_{m})
, their energy constrained diamond norm distance is defined as

(84)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{N}-\mathcal{M}\|_{\diamond}^{E}\coloneqq\sup_{\mathcal{H}_{r}}\,\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\rho\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{H}_{m}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{r})\\
\tr[\rho_{m}N_{m}\big]\leq E\end{subarray}}\,\big\|(\mathcal{N}-\mathcal{M})\otimes{\textrm{id}}_{R}(\rho)\big\|_{1}\,,

where
N_{m}\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{m}\,a_{i}^{\dagger}a_{i}
denotes the total photon number operator, and where the supremum is over all bipartite states
\rho_{mr}
on
\mathcal{H}_{\otimes}\mathcal{H}_{r}
with reduced state
\rho_{m}
on
\mathcal{H}_{m}
of average total photon number at most
E
, for some arbitrary separable Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{r}
.

Not that we denote the set of quantum states over a separable Hilbert space as
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{H}):=\{\rho\in\mathcal{T}_{1,\operatorname{sa}}(\mathcal{H})\;:\;\rho\geq 0,\tr[\rho]=1\}
in this section.
In other words, the energy-constrained diamond norm is a measure of distinguishability between quantum channels with entanglement assistance, and where the input states used for this task are restricted to a physically relevant set of energy-limited states. By the same reasoning as for the usual diamond norm, the supremum in (
84
) can be restricted to
\mathcal{H}_{r}\cong\mathcal{H}_{m}
, and the optimization can be restricted to pure states on
\mathcal{H}_{m}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{r}
. Moreover, it turns out that the above definition is equivalent to one where the input state is energy limited on both
m
and
r
, as introduced in
[
55
]
:

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{N}-\mathcal{M}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\diamond}^{E}\coloneqq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\rho\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{H}_{m}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{m^{\prime}})\\
\tr[\rho(N_{m}+N_{m^{\prime}})\big]\leq E\end{subarray}}\,\big\|(\mathcal{N}-\mathcal{M})\otimes{\textrm{id}}_{m^{\prime}}(\rho)\big\|_{1}\,,

for
\mathcal{H}_{m^{\prime}}\cong\mathcal{H}_{m}
, and where the difference with (
84
) lies in the input states in the above optimization are energy constrained in both their inputs. Clearly, from the definitions we have that

(85)

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{N}-\mathcal{M}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\diamond}^{E}\leq\|\mathcal{N}-\mathcal{M}\|_{\diamond}^{E}\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{N}-\mathcal{M}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\diamond}^{2E}\,.

The second bound above simply results from taking a state
\rho_{mm^{\prime}}
for which
\tr[\rho_{m}N_{m}\big]\leq E
and unitarily rotating the second register so that
\rho_{m}^{\prime}=\rho_{m}
, and therefore
\tr[\rho_{m^{\prime}}N_{m^{\prime}}\big]\leq E
, too.

Similarly, in the next lemma, we establish a straightforward connection between the energy-constrained diamond norm distance between two channels and certain norms between Sobolev spaces:

Lemma 5.5

For any two completely positive, trace preserving maps
\mathcal{N},\mathcal{M}:\mathcal{T}_{1}(\mathcal{H}_{1})\to\mathcal{T}_{1}(\mathcal{H}_{1})
and
E\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{N}-\mathcal{M}\|_{\diamond}^{E}=(1+E)\,\sup_{\rho\in\mathcal{D}}\,\frac{\|(\mathcal{N}-\mathcal{M})\otimes\operatorname{id}_{M^{\prime}}(\rho)\|_{1}}{\|\rho\|_{W^{(2,0),1}}}\,,

where
\mathcal{D}\coloneqq\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{H}_{m}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{m^{\prime}})
above. Similar identities hold in multi-mode settings.

Proof.

This is direct from

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{N}-\mathcal{M}}\|_{\diamond}^{E}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\rho\in\mathcal{D}\\
\tr[\rho N]\leq E\end{subarray}}\,\|(\mathcal{N}-\mathcal{M})\otimes{\textrm{id}}(\rho)\|_{1}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\rho\in\mathcal{D}\\
\|\rho\|_{W^{(2,0),1}}\leq 1+E\end{subarray}}\,\|(\mathcal{N}-\mathcal{M})\otimes{\textrm{id}}(\rho)\|_{1}\,.

∎

Lemma
5.5
